D5&$a$&$a$&$a$&$a$&$a$&$a^{-2}$&$a^{-2}$&$a^{-2}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(3.4)

The index for the theory in (
3.3
) is then given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.99}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}=\frac{1}{(N!)^{3}}\sum_{\{m_{1}^{(\alpha)}\},\{m_{2}^{(\alpha)}\},\{m_{3}^{(\alpha)}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{N}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{i})^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}w_{i}^{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{i=1}^{3}Z_{\text{vec}}^{\U(N)}(x;\{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}\})\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{N}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i}u^{\alpha}_{i}(u^{\beta}_{i})^{-1};m^{(\alpha)}_{i}-m^{(\beta)}_{i}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{N}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{i}(b_{i}u^{(\alpha)}_{i}(u^{(\beta)}_{i+1})^{-1})^{s};s(m^{(\alpha)}_{i}-m^{(\beta)}_{i+1})\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\prod_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{j}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{N}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{j}((f^{(\rho_{j})}_{j})^{-1}u^{(\alpha)}_{j})^{s};sm^{(\alpha)}_{j}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.5)

where the Chern-Simons levels are

\begin{split}\kappa_{1}=k~,\qquad\kappa_{2}=-k~,\qquad\kappa_{3}=k~.\end{split}

(3.6)

For definiteness, we take
k>0
throughout this discussion. In the above, the fugacities
b_{i}
are turned on only for the sake of convenience in keeping track of each bifundamental hypermultiplet, and can be absorbed in the redefinition of the fugacities for topological symmetries
w_{i}
.

The goodness condition for this theory is

F_{1}+F_{2}+k\geq\frac{N}{2}~,\quad F_{2}+F_{3}+k\geq\frac{N}{2}~,

(3.7)

which ensures that no monopole operator violates the unitarity bound.

The Hilbert series for each branch is obtained by first performing the redefinition

x=\mathfrak{z}t~,\quad a=\mathfrak{z}^{-1/2}t^{1/2}~,

(3.8)

and then taking a specific limit of the index:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[\text{branch}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\};\{f_{i}\})=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}\Big|_{\eqref{branchT3label},\,\eqref{ex1fugred}}~.\end{split}

(3.9)

3.1.1
Abelian case:
N=1

Let us focus on the
N=1
case, where the index (
3.5
) simplifies to:

\begin{split}\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivT3axial},N=1}=&\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{i}^{\kappa_{i}m_{i}}w_{i}^{m_{i}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i};0\right)\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{i}(b_{i}u_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s};s(m_{i}-m_{i+1})\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{j=1}^{3}\prod_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{j}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{j}((f_{j}^{(\rho_{j})})^{-1}u_{j})^{s};sm_{j}\right)~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.10)

To compute the limit, we first replace the definition of the chiral multiplet contribution, given in (
2.4
), and, for each assignment of axial fugacities in (
3.4
), we collect an overall exponential
\mathfrak{z}
factor.
A non-vanishing result in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit imposes the following constraints on the magnetic fluxes
m_{i}
:

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{c|c|c}\hline\cr Branch&Power of $\mathfrak{z}$&Condition\\
\hline\cr NS5&$\mathfrak{z}^{|m_{1}-m_{2}|+\frac{F_{3}}{2}|m_{3}|}$&$m_{1}=m_{2}$, $m_{3}=0$\\
$(1,k)$&$\mathfrak{z}^{|m_{2}-m_{3}|+\frac{F_{1}}{2}|m_{1}|}$&$m_{2}=m_{3}$, $m_{1}=0$\\
D5&$\mathfrak{z}^{|m_{1}-m_{2}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+\sum_{i=1}^{3}F_{i}|m_{i}|}$&$m_{1}=m_{2}=m_{3}=0$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(3.11)

We now discuss the computation for each branch explicitly.

The NS5-branch

The limit of the index for the NS5-branch is

\begin{split}&H[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\to 0}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{z}^{|m_{1}-m_{2}|+\frac{F_{3}}{2}|m_{3}|}t^{2\Delta(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\oint\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{i}^{\kappa_{i}m_{i}}w_{i}^{m_{i}}PE\left[\delta_{m_{1},m_{2}}t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{1}u_{1}u_{2}^{-1})^{s}\right]~.\end{split}

(3.12)

Here, the argument of the plethystic exponential only includes contributions from chiral fields with axial fugacity
a
, as the contribution of a chiral field with axial fugacity
a^{-1}
becomes trivial in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit (see (
2.8
) and (
2.11
)).
The function
\Delta(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})
is the conformal dimension of a bare monopole:

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=\frac{1}{2}(F_{1}|m_{1}|+|m_{1}-m_{2}|+F_{2}|m_{2}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+F_{3}|m_{3}|)~.\end{split}

(3.13)

In the limit
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
, only terms carrying zero power of
\mathfrak{z}
can contribute, which imposes the condition on the magnetic fluxes:
m_{1}=m_{2}
and
m_{3}=0
12
12

12

As a technical aside, if
F_{3}
is set to zero, then no condition on
m_{3}
arises. However, the integral over
u_{3}
now simply reads

\oint\frac{du_{3}u_{3}^{k\,m_{3}}}{2\pi iu_{3}}=\delta_{m_{3},0}~,

(3.14)

thus forcing
m_{3}
to be zero. This argument holds for any value
F_{3}\neq 0
regardless of the condition imposed by the power of
\mathfrak{z}
.
. We thus obtain the following expression for the Hilbert series:

\begin{split}\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{m_{1}=m_{2}=m\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=m,\,m_{2}=m,\,m_{3}=0)}\\
&\qquad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\frac{du_{2}}{2\pi iu_{2}}\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}u_{1}^{\kappa_{1}m_{1}}u_{2}^{\kappa_{2}m_{2}}w_{1}^{m_{1}}w_{2}^{m_{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{1}u_{1}u_{2}^{-1})^{s}\right]\\
&\overset{\eqref{usefulintiden}}{=}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{(F_{1}+F_{2}+1+k)|m|}\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}y_{1}^{m}~,\qquad y_{1}=b_{1}^{-k}w_{1}w_{2}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(y_{1}+y_{1}^{-1})t^{F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1}-t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1)}\right]~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.15)

In the first step, we recognise that the integral over
u_{3}
yields one, while the integral over
u_{1,2}
is non-trivial and evaluates to the function
t^{k|m|}y_{1}^{m}(1-t^{2})^{-1}
upon applying identity (
A.1
). The
t
factor at the numerator adds up to the conformal dimension of the bare monopole operator and represents the contribution of the dressing of bifundamental fields, so that the final power of
t
proportional to
|m|
is (twice) the conformal dimension of a gauge invariant dressed monopole operator. The denominator instead is analogous to a
P
-factor in the language of
21
.
The final expression in (
3.15
) is the Hilbert series for the orbifold
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1}
. As shown in the preceding line, this matches the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of a U(1) gauge theory with
F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1
flavours, which is the proposed magnetic quiver for this branch
50
.

The elementary bare monopole operators on this branch,
V_{\pm(1,1,0)}
, have an R-charge of
\frac{1}{2}(F_{1}+F_{2}+1)
and axial charge of
F_{1}+F_{2}+1
. The generators of this branch are

\begin{split}G_{0}=X_{12}X_{21}~,\quad G_{+}=X_{21}^{k}V_{(1,1,0)}~,\quad G_{-}=X_{12}^{k}V_{(-1,-1,0)}~,\end{split}

(3.16)

which satisfy the relation

\begin{split}G_{+}G_{-}=G_{0}^{F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1}~,\end{split}

(3.17)

where
X_{12}
and
X_{21}
are the chiral and antichiral fields in the bifundamental hypermultiplet connecting the first and second gauge nodes, respectively.
13
13

13

Here and throughout the paper, we denote by
X_{ij}
the chiral field in the bifundamental hypermultiplet going from the
i
-th gauge node to the
j
-th gauge node. This is conventional in the literature of quiver gauge theories.

The
(1,k)
-branch

The result for this branch can be obtained from that of the NS5-branch by exchanging the nodes
1
and
3
:

\begin{split}\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[(1,k)](t;\{w_{i}\},\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{m_{2}=m_{3}=m\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=0,\,m_{2}=m,\,m_{3}=m)}\\
&\qquad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\frac{du_{2}}{2\pi iu_{2}}\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}u_{2}^{\kappa_{2}m_{2}}u_{3}^{\kappa_{3}m_{3}}w_{2}^{m_{2}}w_{3}^{m_{3}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{2}u_{2}u_{3}^{-1})^{s}\right]\\
&\overset{\eqref{usefulintiden}}{=}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{(F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1)|m|}\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}y^{m}~,\qquad y_{2}=b_{2}^{k}w_{2}w_{3}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(y_{2}+y_{2}^{-1})t^{F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1}-t^{2(F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1)}\right]~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.18)

This is the Hilbert series for
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1}
, matching the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of a U(1) gauge theory with
F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1
flavours.

The generators of this branch are analogous to those of the NS5-branch, with the replacements
V_{\pm(1,1,0)}\to V_{\pm(0,1,1)}
and
(X_{12},X_{21})\to(X_{32},X_{23})
.

The D5-branch

Finally, assuming that
F_{1}\neq 0
and
F_{3}\neq 0
, the Hilbert series for the D5-branch is
14
14

14

Notice that this assumption is sufficient, but not necessary, to have a good theory. For cases in which
F_{1}=0
and/or
F_{3}=0
, we need an improved strategy to compute the D5-branch Hilbert series. We leave this analysis for a future work.

\begin{split}\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[\text{D5}](t;b_{1,2,3})=\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\frac{du_{2}}{2\pi iu_{2}}\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{2}(b_{i}u_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{j}}(f^{(\rho_{j})}_{j}u_{j}^{-1})^{s}\right\}-3t^{2}\right]~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.19)

The term
-3t^{2}
in the plethystic exponential accounts for the three adjoint chiral multiplets, which have an axial fugacity of
a^{-2}
. This result corresponds to the Higgs branch Hilbert series of the theory in (
3.1
) with the Chern-Simons levels set to zero (
k=0
), which is the 3d
{\cal N}=4
theory:

1
1
1
F_{1}
F_{2}
F_{3}

(3.20)

The magnetic quiver for this branch is simply the mirror theory for this quiver. For example, in the special case of
F_{1}=1
,
F_{2}=0
, and
F_{3}=1
, (
3.20
) is mirror dual to the magnetic quiver consisting of the
\U(1)
gauge theory with four flavours
(
50
, Section 5.1.1)
.

In summary, the results for each branch are:

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{c|c|c}\hline\cr Branch&Hilbert series&Geometry\\
\hline\cr NS5&\eqref{HSNSabel}&$\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1}$\\
$(1,k)$&\eqref{1kT3abel}&$\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F_{2}+F_{3}+k+1}$\\
D5&\eqref{HSD5T3abel}&Higgs branch of \eqref{elecquivD5}\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(3.21)

These Hilbert series can also be derived using the methods presented in
22
; see Appendix C therein for related examples.

3.1.2
The case of
N=2

We now turn to the non-Abelian case of
N=2
.

The NS5-branch

Following a similar analysis as in the Abelian case, we compute the limit

\begin{split}&H[\text{NS5}](t,\{w_{i}\},\{b_{i}\})=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\to 0}\sum_{\{\{m_{1}^{(\alpha)}\},\{m_{2}^{(\alpha)}\},\{m_{3}^{(\alpha)}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{z}^{\Delta_{\mathfrak{z}}(\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\bm{m}_{3})}t^{2\Delta(\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\bm{m}_{3})}\\
&\quad\times\oint\prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{\alpha=1,2}\frac{du_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{i}^{(\alpha)}}\left(u_{i}^{(\alpha)}\right)^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}}w_{i}^{m_{i}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{1\leq\alpha\neq\beta\leq 2}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\left(1-\frac{u_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{u_{i}^{(\beta)}}\right)^{\delta_{m_{i}^{(\alpha)},m_{i}^{(\beta)}}}\\
&\quad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}\delta_{m_{1}^{(\alpha)},m_{2}^{(\beta)}}(b_{1}u_{1}^{(\alpha)})^{s}(u_{2}^{(\beta)})^{-s}\right]~.\end{split}

(3.22)

The argument of the plethystic exponential only includes contributions from chiral fields with axial fugacity
a
. The function
\Delta(\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\bm{m}_{3})
is the conformal dimension of the bare monopole operator:

\begin{split}\begin{split}\Delta(\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\bm{m}_{3})&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{3}F_{j}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}|m^{(\alpha)}_{j}|+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}(|m^{(\alpha)}_{1}-m^{(\beta)}_{2}|\\
&\qquad+|m^{(\alpha)}_{2}-m^{(\beta)}_{3}|)-2\sum_{i=1}^{3}|m^{(1)}_{i}-m^{(2)}_{i}|\Big)~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.23)

The condition for a non-vanishing limit requires the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
to be zero:

\begin{split}&\Delta_{\mathfrak{z}}(\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\bm{m}_{3})\\
&=\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}|m^{(\alpha)}_{1}-m^{(\beta)}_{2}|+\frac{F_{3}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}|m^{(\alpha)}_{3}|-\sum_{i=1}^{3}|m^{(1)}_{i}-m^{(2)}_{i}|=0~.\end{split}

(3.24)

We observe that the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
might be negative, which happens for
F_{3}=0
, and this could lead to divergencies in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit. However, notice that the
u_{3}
integral, for generic values of the magnetic fluxes and in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit, reads

\frac{1}{2}\oint\prod_{\alpha=1,2}\frac{du_{3}^{(\alpha)}(u_{3}^{(\alpha)})^{k\,m_{3}^{(\alpha)}}}{2\pi iu_{3}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-(u_{3}^{(1)})^{s}(u_{3}^{(2)})^{-s}\right]=\delta_{\mathbf{m}_{3},0}~,

(3.25)

thus setting
\mathbf{m}_{3}
to zero and avoiding possible negative powers of
\mathfrak{z}
.
15
15

15

This is a general feature. Whenever a gauge node sees only hypermultiplets with fugacity
a^{-1}
or
a^{0}
, then its integral can be solved to effectively set its corresponding magnetic flux to zero. As expected, gauge nodes that are distant from those that are involved in the construction of dressed monopole operators do not play any role.
Then we just need to look for zero powers of
\mathfrak{z}
, which give a non-vanishing contribution to the limit
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
.
All in all, we restrict the magnetic fluxes to be of the form
\bm{m}_{3}=(0,0)
and
\bm{m}_{2}=\bm{m}_{3}=(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Furthermore, using the Weyl symmetry, we can impose
\mathfrak{m}\geq\mathfrak{n}
.

Taking the limit (
3.9
), the Hilbert series receives two distinct contributions, analogous to the examples in
(
22
, Appendix C)
, namely one from fluxes where
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}
, and another from fluxes where
\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}
:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[\text{NS5}](t;w_{1,2};b_{1,2})=H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}+H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(3.26)

For fluxes with
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}
, each U(2) gauge group is unbroken, and the contribution is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.97}{$\begin{split}&H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{2^{2}}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\left(\frac{u^{(1)}_{1}u_{1}^{(2)}}{u_{2}^{(1)}u_{2}^{(2)}}\right)^{k\mathfrak{m}}(w_{1}w_{2})^{2\mathfrak{m}}t^{2\Delta(\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}),\,\bm{m}_{3}=\bm{0})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{1\leq\alpha\neq\beta\leq 2}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\Bigg(1-\frac{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{u^{(\beta)}_{i}}\Bigg)\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}(b_{1}u^{(\alpha)}_{1})^{s}(u^{(\beta)}_{2})^{-s}\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.27)

We can simplify (
3.27
) by explicitly evaluating the integral using identity (
A.2
), which leads to

\begin{split}H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{k(2|\mathfrak{m}|)}(b_{1}^{-k}w_{1}w_{2})^{2\mathfrak{m}}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}\times t^{(F_{1}+F_{2}+2)(2|\mathfrak{m}|)}\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2)(2|\mathfrak{m}|)}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}(b_{1}^{-k}w_{1}w_{2})^{2\mathfrak{m}}~.\end{split}

(3.28)

Notice that the evaluation of the integral, which is the ratio in the first line, has two contributions. The
t
exponential factor modifies the dimension of the bare monopole operator, so that it is included the contribution from the dressing. Also, the denominator can be interpreted as a
P
-factor in the language of
21
; see also (
A.3
).

For fluxes with
\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}
, each U(2) gauge group is broken to its maximal torus U(1)
2
. The corresponding contribution to the Hilbert series is

\begin{split}H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\,{\cal H}(\mathfrak{m})\,{\cal H}(\mathfrak{n})\,\,t^{2\Delta(\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}),\bm{m}_{3}=\bm{0})}~,\end{split}

(3.29)

where
{\cal H}(m)
is the integral part of the
N=1
result from (
3.15
):

\begin{split}{\cal H}(m)&=\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\frac{du_{2}}{2\pi iu_{2}}u_{1}^{\kappa_{1}m}u_{2}^{\kappa_{2}m}w_{1}^{m}w_{2}^{m}\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{1}u_{1}u_{2}^{-1})^{s}\right]\\
&=\frac{t^{k|m|}}{1-t^{2}}(b_{1}^{-k}w_{1}w_{2})^{m}~.\end{split}

(3.30)

Notice again that the result gives a
t
exponential factor, that accounts for the dressing of bare monopoles, and a
P
-factor.
Therefore, we have

\begin{split}H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}\frac{t^{(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2)(|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{n}|)-2|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}(b_{1}^{-k}w_{1}w_{2})^{\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(3.31)

Summing the two contributions as indicated in (
3.26
) yields the total Hilbert series:

\begin{split}\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivT3axial}}[\text{NS5}](t;w_{1,2};b_{1,2})\\
&\quad=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\geq\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2)(|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{n}|)-2|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|}\,y_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}}\,P_{\U(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})\\
&\quad=\mathop{\rm PE}\Big[t^{2}+t^{4}+\left(y_{1}+y_{1}^{-1}\right)(t^{F_{1}+F_{2}+k}+t^{F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2})\\
&\qquad\qquad-t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+1)}-t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2)}\Big]~,\quad y_{1}=b_{1}^{-1}w_{1}w_{2}~,\end{split}\end{split}

(3.32)

where the
P
-factor is defined as in (
A.3
).
This result matches the Coulomb branch Hilbert series for the proposed magnetic quiver
50
, which is a U(2) gauge theory with
F_{1}+F_{2}+k+2
flavours. Notice that the
P_{\U(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})
factor does not depend on the specific value of
(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})
, but only on whether
\mathfrak{m}
is equal or not to
\mathfrak{n}
. This agreement follows from the structure of the Coulomb branch formula in
21
. For
m^{(1)}=m^{(2)}
, the U(2) gauge group is unbroken, and the factor
P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n})=\left[(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})\right]^{-1}
is the character for the Casimir invariants. For
\mathfrak{m}\neq\mathfrak{n}
, the group is broken to U(1)
2
, and
P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m}\neq\mathfrak{n})=(1-t^{2})^{-2}
accounts for the Casimirs of the residual symmetry.

The
(1,k)
-branch

The analysis for this branch is identical to that of the NS5-branch, with the nodes
1
and
3
exchanged (and
b_{1}
replaced by
b_{3}
).

The D5-branch

We now compute the D5-branch, assuming
F_{1}>1
and
F_{3}>1
.
16
16

16

As in the Abelian case, this condition is sufficient, but not necessary, for the theory to be good. We will however leave the analysis for more general values of the flavours to a future work.

As in the Abelian case, the magnetic fluxes are constrained to be
\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=\bm{m}_{3}=\bm{0}
, meaning that monopole operators do not contribute to this branch.
The resulting Hilbert series is therefore the Higgs branch series of the corresponding theory with zero Chern-Simons levels (
k=0
):

2
2
2
F_{1}
F_{2}
F_{3}

(3.33)

The magnetic quiver for this branch is simply the mirror theory for this quiver.

3.2
The
T_{(p,q)}[3]
theory and its generalisation

Let us consider the following brane system:

N
D3
NS5
NS5
(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
D5
D5

(3.34)

This system and the corresponding field theory, known as the
T_{(p,q)}[3]
theory, were considered in
(
7
, Section 7)
. We take
p=2\mathfrak{p}
and
q=2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1
, such that
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. We define the generators of
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})
as follows:

\begin{split}\mathrm{S}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}~,\qquad\mathrm{T}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}~,\end{split}

(3.35)

where
\mathrm{S}^{2}=-1
and
(\mathrm{S}\mathrm{T})^{3}=1
. We define

\begin{split}J=-\mathrm{T}^{\mathfrak{q}}\mathrm{S}\mathrm{T}^{2\mathfrak{p}}~.\end{split}

(3.36)

Since
J_{k}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\end{pmatrix}^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}2\mathfrak{p}&2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1\end{pmatrix}^{t}
, it follows that each
(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
-brane can be regarded an NS5-brane between a
J
wall on the left and a
J^{-1}
wall on the right (see
6
). Note that the
\mathrm{T}^{\kappa}
wall gives rise to the gauge group
\U(N)_{\kappa}
, the
\mathrm{S}
wall gives rise to the
T[\U(N)]
theory, and the
\mathrm{S}^{-1}
wall gives rise to the
\overline{T[\U(N)]}
theory.
17
17

17

As defined in
29
, the
T[\U(1)]
theory is the supersymmetric completion of the Chern-Simons coupling
\frac{1}{2\pi}\int F\wedge dW
between two
\U(1)
gauge fields
F
and
W
, where each of them resides in an
{\cal N}=4
vector multiplet. The
T[\U(N)]
theory is defined as the product of the
T[\mathrm{SU}(N)]
theory and the
T[\U(1)]
theory, in such a way that the
\mathfrak{su}(N)\times\mathfrak{su}(N)
global symmetry of
T[\mathrm{SU}(N)]
is promoted to
\mathfrak{u}(N)\times\mathfrak{u}(N)
with the mixed Chern-Simons term due to the
T[\U(1)]
theory. In terms of the index, if we denote by
\{(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{N}),(m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{N})\}
the flavour fugacities and corresponding magnectic fluxes, and by
\{(w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{N}),(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{N})\}
the topological fugacities and corresponding magnectic fluxes, then the
T[\U(1)]
contribution to the index is
(f_{1}f_{2}\cdots f_{N})^{n_{N}}(w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{N})^{m_{N}}
. The
\overline{T[\mathrm{SU}(N)]}
theory can be contain from
T[\U(N)]
by setting
f_{j}\rightarrow f_{j}^{-1}
and
m_{j}\rightarrow-m_{j}
, for all
j=1,\ldots,N
.
The corresponding theory is therefore

N_{\mathfrak{q}}
N_{2\mathfrak{p}}
N_{-2\mathfrak{p}}
N_{-\mathfrak{q}}
N_{\mathfrak{q}}
N_{2\mathfrak{p}}
1
1
T[\U(N)]
\overline{T[\U(N)]}
T[\U(N)]

(3.37)

3.2.1
The Abelian case

Let us focus on the case of
N=1
. In order to compute the limit of the index, we assign the following axial fugacities to the chiral fields in the quiver.

1_{\mathfrak{q}}
1_{2\mathfrak{p}}
1_{-2\mathfrak{p}}
1_{-\mathfrak{q}}
1_{\mathfrak{q}}
1_{2\mathfrak{p}}
1
1
T[\U(1)]
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
\overline{T[\U(1)]}
\mathfrak{a}_{4}
T[\U(1)]
\mathfrak{c}_{1}
\mathfrak{c}_{5}
\mathfrak{b}_{1}
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
\mathfrak{b}_{3}
\mathfrak{b}_{4}
\mathfrak{b}_{5}
\mathfrak{b}_{6}

(3.38)

The index of (
3.38
) is then

\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivpqaxialabel}}=\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{6}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{i}^{\kappa_{i}m_{i}}w_{i}^{m_{i}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i};0\right)\Bigg)\\
&\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=2,3}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{i}(u_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s};sm_{i}-sm_{i+1}\right)\prod_{j=1,5}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{j}(f^{-1}_{j}u_{j})^{s};sm_{j}\right)\\
&\times(u_{1}^{m_{2}}u_{2}^{m_{1}})(u_{3}^{-m_{4}}u_{4}^{-m_{3}})(u_{5}^{m_{6}}u_{6}^{m_{5}})~,\end{split}

(3.39)

where

\begin{split}\kappa_{1}=\mathfrak{q}~,\,\,\,\,\kappa_{2}=2\mathfrak{p}~,\,\,\,\,\kappa_{3}=-2\mathfrak{p}~,\,\,\,\,\kappa_{4}=-\mathfrak{q}~,\,\,\,\,\kappa_{5}=\mathfrak{q}~,\,\,\,\,\kappa_{6}=2\mathfrak{p}~,\end{split}

(3.40)

and for each branch the axial fugacities are as follows:

\begin{split}&\begin{tabular}[]{c|ccccccccccc}\hline\cr Branch&$\mathfrak{a}_{2}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{4}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{5}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{2}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{3}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{4}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{5}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{6}$\\
\hline\cr NS5&$a$&$a^{-1}$&$a^{-1}$&1&1&1&1&1&1&1\\
$(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)$&$a^{-1}$&$a$&$1$&$a^{-1}$&$1$&1&1&1&1&1\\
D5&$a$&$a$&$a$&$a$&$a^{-2}$&$a^{-2}$&$1$&$1$&$a^{-2}$&$1$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(3.41)

We can now analyse each branch separately.

The NS5-branch

On the right hand side of (
3.9
), we find that the power of
\mathfrak{z}
is
|m_{2}-m_{3}|+\frac{1}{2}|m_{5}|
. Thus, a non-vanishing result in the limit
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
restricts

\begin{split}m_{2}=m_{3}=\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}~,\qquad m_{5}=0~.\end{split}

(3.42)

The dimension of the monopole operators is

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1},\ldots,m_{5})=\frac{1}{2}(|m_{1}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+|m_{4}-m_{5}|+|m_{5}|)~,\end{split}

(3.43)

and the limit of the index for the NS5-branch reads

\begin{split}\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{m_{1},\mathfrak{m},m_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1},\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m},m_{4},0)}w_{1}^{m_{1}}(w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{4}^{m_{4}}\\
&\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{5}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{1}^{m_{1}\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{m}}u_{2}^{2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}+m_{1}}u_{3}^{-2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}-m_{4}}u_{4}^{-m_{4}\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{m}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{2}u_{2}u_{3}^{-1})^{s}\right]~.\end{split}\end{split}

(3.44)

The integrals over
u_{1}
and
u_{4}
impose the following conditions:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{m}=-m_{1}\mathfrak{q}~,\,\,\mathfrak{m}=-m_{4}\mathfrak{q}\quad\Rightarrow\quad m_{1}=m_{4}=\mathfrak{n}~,\,\,\mathfrak{m}=-\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{q}~,\end{split}

(3.45)

whereas the integral over
u_{6}
and the magnetic flux
m_{6}
are neglected for the reason explained in footnote
15
. The expression in (
3.44
) reduces to

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(\mathfrak{n},-\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{n},0)}\left(\frac{w_{1}w_{4}}{w_{2}^{\mathfrak{q}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\,\,\,\,\times\left(\prod_{j=2}^{3}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{2}^{-(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)\mathfrak{n}}u_{3}^{(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)\mathfrak{n}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{2}u_{2}u_{3}^{-1})^{s}\right]~,\end{split}

(3.46)

from which we see that the “effective Chern-Simons levels” for the second and third nodes are now
-(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
and
(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
, respectively. To form the gauge invariant quantities, we need to dress bare the monopole operator
V_{(1,-\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{q},1,0,0)}
associated with
\mathfrak{n}=1
with
X_{23}
and
V_{(-1,\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{q},-1,0,0)}
associated with
\mathfrak{n}=-1
with
X_{32}
. Similarly to (
3.15
), we obtain

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})&\overset{\eqref{usefulintiden}}{=}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{|\mathfrak{n}|[1+1+(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)]}\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}y^{\mathfrak{n}}~,\quad y=b_{2}^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}\frac{w_{1}w_{4}}{w_{2}^{\mathfrak{q}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(y+y^{-1})t^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+1}-t^{2(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+1)}\right]~.\end{split}

(3.47)

This is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+1}
, in agreement with
(
7
, (7.1))
. This space can be realised as the Coulomb branch of the magnetic quiver described by the
\U(1)
gauge theory with
2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+1
flavours
(
50
, (5.12))
. In summary, this branch is generated by

\begin{split}G_{0}=X_{23}X_{32}~,\quad G_{+}=V_{(1,-\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{q},1,0,0)}X_{23}^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}~,\quad G_{-}=V_{-(1,-\mathfrak{q},-\mathfrak{q},1,0,0)}X_{32}^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}~,\end{split}

(3.48)

satisfying
G_{0}^{4\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+2}=G_{+}G_{-}
.

The
(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
-branch

In this case, we have
\mathfrak{z}^{\frac{1}{2}|m_{1}|+|m4-m5|}
. In the limit of
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
, a non-vanishing result imposes the conditions

\begin{split}m_{4}=m_{5}=\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}~,\qquad m_{1}=0~.\end{split}

(3.49)

The limit (
3.9
) of the index in this case reads

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{m_{3},\mathfrak{m},m_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(0,m_{2},m_{3},\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m})}w_{3}^{m_{3}}(w_{4}w_{5})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{6}^{m_{6}}\\
&\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{6}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{1}^{m_{2}}u_{2}^{2m_{2}\mathfrak{p}}u_{3}^{-2m_{3}\mathfrak{p}-\mathfrak{m}}u_{4}^{-m_{3}-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{m}}u_{5}^{m_{6}+\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{m}}u_{6}^{2m_{6}\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{m}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{4}u_{4}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.50)

The integrations over
u_{1}
,
u_{2}
,
u_{3}
,
u_{6}
impose

\begin{split}m_{2}=0~,\quad m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}~,\quad m_{6}=\mathfrak{n}~,\quad\mathfrak{m}=-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{n}~,\end{split}

(3.51)

from which the expression above simplifies to

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(0,0,\mathfrak{n},-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{n},-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{n})}\left(\frac{w_{3}w_{6}}{w_{4}^{2\mathfrak{p}}w_{5}^{2\mathfrak{p}}}\right)^{\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\,\,\,\,\times\left(\prod_{j=4}^{5}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{4}^{(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)\mathfrak{n}}u_{5}^{-(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)\mathfrak{n}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(b_{4}u_{4}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}\right]~.\end{split}

(3.52)

We thus obtain

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})\\
&\overset{\eqref{usefulintiden}}{=}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{|\mathfrak{n}|[1+2\mathfrak{p}+(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)]}\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}y^{\mathfrak{n}}~,\qquad y=b_{4}^{-(2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)}\frac{w_{3}w_{6}}{w_{4}^{2\mathfrak{p}}w_{5}^{2\mathfrak{p}}}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(y+y^{-1})t^{2\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{q}+1)}-t^{4\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{q}+1)}\right]~,\end{split}

(3.53)

which is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{q}+1)}
, in agreement with
(
7
, (7.1))
. This space can be realised as the Coulomb branch of the magnetic quiver described by the
\U(1)
gauge theory with
2\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{q}+1)
flavours
(
50
, (5.13))
.
In particular, this branch is generated by

\begin{split}G_{0}=X_{45}X_{54}~,\,\,G_{+}=V_{(0,0,1,-2\mathfrak{p},-2\mathfrak{p},1)}X_{45}^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}~,\,\,G_{-}=V_{-(0,0,1,-2\mathfrak{p},-2\mathfrak{p},1)}X_{54}^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}~,\end{split}

(3.54)

satisfying
G_{0}^{8\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+8}=G_{+}G_{-}
. As usual, we denote by
X_{45}
and
X_{54}
the chiral and antichiral fields in the bifundamental hypermultiplet connecting the gauge nodes 4 and 5.

The D5-branch

In this case, we have
\mathfrak{z}^{|m_{1}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+|m_{4}-m_{5}|+|m_{5}|}
. In the limit
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
, a non-vanishing result imposes the conditions

\begin{split}m_{1}=0~,\quad m_{2}=m_{3}=\mathfrak{m}~,\quad m_{4}=m_{5}=0~.\end{split}

(3.55)

The limit (
3.9
) of the index in this case is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[\text{D5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{\mathfrak{m},m_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(0,\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m},0,0)}(w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{6}^{m_{6}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{6}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}u_{2}^{2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}}u_{3}^{-2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}}u_{4}^{-\mathfrak{m}}u_{5}^{m_{6}}u_{6}^{2m_{6}\mathfrak{p}}\\
&\qquad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-3t^{2}+t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\{(f_{1}u_{1}^{-1})^{s}+(u_{2}u_{3}^{-1})^{s}+(u_{4}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}+(f_{5}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}\}\right]~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.56)

where the integral over
u_{6}
sets
m_{6}=0
. We thus obtain

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpqbrane}}[\text{D5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}(w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{m}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{5}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}u_{2}^{2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}}u_{3}^{-2\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}}u_{4}^{-\mathfrak{m}}\\
&\qquad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-3t^{2}+t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\{(f_{1}u_{1}^{-1})^{s}+(b_{2}u_{2}u_{3}^{-1})^{s}+(b_{4}u_{4}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}+(f_{5}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}\}\right]\\
&\overset{\eqref{usefulintiden}}{=}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\prod_{j=1,5}\oint\frac{du_{j}}{2\pi iu_{j}}\right)u_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}u_{5}^{-\mathfrak{m}}\times t^{2\mathfrak{p}|\mathfrak{m}|}(b_{2}^{-2\mathfrak{p}}w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{m}}\times t^{|\mathfrak{m}|}b_{4}^{\mathfrak{m}}\\
&\qquad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-t^{2}+t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\{(f_{1}u_{1}^{-1})^{s}+(f_{5}u_{5}^{-1})^{s}\}\right]\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{(2\mathfrak{p}+3)|\mathfrak{m}|}}{1-t^{2}}y^{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+\left(y+y^{-1}\right)t^{2\mathfrak{p}+3}-t^{4\mathfrak{p}+6}\right]~,\qquad y=f_{1}b_{2}^{-2\mathfrak{p}}b_{4}f_{5}^{-1}w_{2}w_{3}~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.57)

This is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{2\mathfrak{p}+3}
, in agreement with
(
7
, (7.1))
. This space can be realised as the Coulomb branch of the magnetic quiver described by the
\U(1)
gauge theory with
2\mathfrak{p}+3
flavours
(
50
, (5.14))
.

We point out that, in contrast to the previous cases in which the quivers contain only fundamental and bifundamental hypermultiplets, the D5-branch in this theory is generated also by dressed monopole operators. From (
3.57
), we see that such generators are

\begin{split}G_{+}=V_{(0,1,1,0,0,0)}\widetilde{Q}_{1}X_{32}^{2\mathfrak{p}}X_{45}Q_{5}~,\quad G_{+}=V_{-(0,1,1,0,0,0)}Q_{1}X_{23}^{2\mathfrak{p}}X_{54}\widetilde{Q}_{5}~.\end{split}

(3.58)

The other generators can be taken as one among the following combinations:
Q_{1}\widetilde{Q}_{1}
,
X_{23}X_{32}
,
X_{45}X_{54}
and
Q_{5}\widetilde{Q}_{5}
, depending on which adjoint chiral field we turn on in (
3.38
). In particular, the choice given in the last line (
3.41
) sets
Q_{1}\widetilde{Q}_{1}=0
,
X_{23}X_{32}=0
,
X_{45}X_{54}=Q_{5}\widetilde{Q}_{5}
, and so the aforementioned generator can be taken as

\begin{split}G_{0}=X_{45}X_{54}=Q_{5}\widetilde{Q}_{5}~.\end{split}

(3.59)

These generators satisfy the relation
G_{0}^{4\mathfrak{p}+6}=G_{+}G_{-}
.

3.2.2
The non-Abelian case

Let us now generalise the discussion above and analyse the limit of the index for theories with
(p,q)
-branes and non-Abelian nodes. For simplicity, we focus on the
N=2
case, which corresponds to a Chern-Simons theory with interpolating
T[\U(2)]
and
\overline{T[\U(2)]}
SCFTs. Specifically, let us consider the following brane system:

2 D3
NS5
NS5
(2\mathfrak{p},2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1)
L
D5
R
D5

(3.60)

where the associated quiver theory reads

2_{\mathfrak{q}}
2_{2\mathfrak{p}}
2_{-2\mathfrak{p}}
2_{-\mathfrak{q}}
L
R
T[\U(2)]
\overline{T[\U(2)]}

(3.61)

in which the flavour and topological symmetries of the
T[\U(2)]
theory are coupled to
{\cal N}=3

\U(2)
vector multiplets with Chern-Simons levels
\mathfrak{q}
and
2\mathfrak{p}
, respectively. Similarly, the flavour and topological symmetries of the
\overline{T[\U(2)]}
theory are gauged with Chern-Simons levels
-2\mathfrak{p}
and
-\mathfrak{q}
, respectively.
We can assign axial fugacities to the various chiral fields appearing in quiver (
3.61
), which can be depicted in 3d
{\cal N}=2
notation as follows:

2_{\mathfrak{q}}
2_{2\mathfrak{p}}
2_{-2\mathfrak{p}}
2_{-\mathfrak{q}}
L
R
T[\U(2)]
\mathfrak{a}_{1}
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
\overline{T[\U(2)]}
\mathfrak{a}_{3}
\mathfrak{c}_{1}
\mathfrak{c}_{4}
\mathfrak{b}_{1}
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
\mathfrak{b}_{3}
\mathfrak{b}_{4}

(3.62)

The index of theory (
3.62
), compatible with the axial charge assignments, is then given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{(\ref {quivpqTU2axialabel})}=\frac{1}{2^{4}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{3}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{4}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{i})^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}w_{i}^{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{4}Z_{\text{vec}}^{\U(2)}\left(x;\{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}\}\right)\left[\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i}u^{\alpha}_{i}(u^{\beta}_{i})^{-1};m^{(\alpha)}_{i}-m^{(\beta)}_{i}\right)\right]^{\epsilon}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{2}\left(u^{(\alpha)}_{2}(u^{(\beta)}_{3})^{-1}\right)^{s};s\left(m^{(\alpha)}_{2}-m^{(\beta)}_{3}\right)\right)\\
&\qquad\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{j=\{1,4\}}\prod_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{j}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{j}\left(u^{(\alpha)}_{j}(f^{(\rho_{j})}_{j})^{-1}\right)^{s};sm^{(\alpha)}_{j}\right)\\
&\qquad\quad\times Z_{T[\U(2)]}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{1}|u^{(\alpha)}_{1};m^{(\alpha)}_{1}|u^{(\alpha)}_{2};m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)Z_{\overline{T[\U(2)]}}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{3}|u^{(\alpha)}_{3};m^{(\alpha)}_{3}|u^{(\alpha)}_{4};m^{(\alpha)}_{4}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.63)

where

\begin{split}\kappa_{1}=\mathfrak{q},\,\,\,\,\kappa_{2}=2\mathfrak{p},\,\,\,\,\kappa_{3}=-2\mathfrak{p},\,\,\,\,\kappa_{4}=-\mathfrak{q}~.\end{split}

(3.64)

For definiteness, we take
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}>0
. We denote by
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{i},m^{(\alpha)}_{i}\}
, with
\alpha=1,2
, the fugacities and magnetic fluxes for the
i
-th
\U(2)
gauge node, where
i=1,\ldots,4
, and with
f^{(\rho_{j})}_{j}
the fugacities for the
j
-th flavour node, with
\rho_{1}=L
and
\rho_{2}=R
. The index contribution due to to the
T[\U(2)]
theory is

\begin{split}&Z_{T[\U(2)]}(x;y|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})\\
&=\sum_{l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times Z^{\U(1)}_{\text{adj}}(x;y)Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(x;y|z;l_{z}|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f})~,\end{split}

(3.65)

where the term highlighted in
violet
is the index of the
T[\U(1)]
theory, and we denote with
(z,l_{z})
,
(\bm{f},\bm{m}_{f})
and
(\bm{w},\bm{n}_{w})
the fugacities and magnetic fluxes for the
\U(1)
gauge group, the flavour and the topological symmetry, respectively. Furthermore, the adjoint chiral field in the
\U(1)
vector multiplet and the hypermultiplet in the bifundamental representation of
\U(1)\times\U(2)
contribute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)}_{\text{adj}}(x;y)&=Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;y^{-2};0\right)~,\end{split}

(3.66a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(x;y|z;l_{z}|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f})&=\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{j=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;y;z^{s}f_{j}^{-s};sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{j}\right)~,\end{split}

(3.66b)

respectively. The index of the
\overline{T[\U(2)]}
theory is then defined as

\begin{split}Z_{\overline{T[\U(2)]}}(x;y|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})=Z_{T[\U(2)]}(x;y|\bm{f}^{-1};-\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})~,\end{split}

(3.67)

with
\{\bm{f}^{-1},-\bm{m}_{f}\}=\{(f_{1}^{-1},f_{2}^{-1}),(-{m_{f}}_{1},-{m_{f}}_{2})\}
.

Subsequently, we focus on the NS5-branch, in which case the axial fugacities and the parameter
\epsilon
, which appears in the second line of (
3.63
), are assigned as follows:

\begin{split}&\begin{tabular}[]{c|ccccccccccc}\hline\cr Branch&$\mathfrak{a}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{2}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{3}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{4}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{2}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{3}$&$\mathfrak{b}_{4}$&$\epsilon$\\
\hline\cr NS5&$a$&$a$&$a^{-1}$&$a^{-1}$&$a^{-1}$&1&1&1&1&0\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(3.68)

The limit of the index in question is

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpqbraneTU2}}[\text{NS5}](t)=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{\cal I}_{(\ref {quivpqTU2axialabel})}\Big|_{\eqref{axialfTU2quiv},\,\eqref{N=3reparam}}~.\end{split}

(3.69)

Limit of the
T[\U(2)]
index

Let us consider the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory, depicted as

2
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{f},\bm{m}_{f}
2
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\bm{w},\bm{n}_{w}
T[\U(2)]
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}y

(3.70)

whose index is given by (
3.65
), with
y
taking values in
\{a,a^{-1}\}
. Observe that the variables
\mathfrak{z}
and
t
defined in (
2.13
) are swapped upon sending
a\rightarrow a^{-1}
. As a consequence, the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit in the theory with axial fugacity
a^{-1}
is equivalent to taking the
t\rightarrow 0
limit in the theory with axial fugacity
a
, and then redefining
\mathfrak{z}=t
. Using the self-mirror property of
T[\U(2)]
, we thus obtain

\begin{split}Z^{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z};t|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})=Z^{t\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(t;\mathfrak{z}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w}|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f})~.\end{split}

(3.71)

The derivation of each of such limits is presented in Appendix
B
. We report the important results as follows:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.86}{$\begin{split}&Z^{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z};t|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})\\
&=\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}\\
&\quad\times\frac{\Theta({n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2})\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)+\Theta({n_{w}}_{2}-{n_{w}}_{1}-1)\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2},{n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}\\
&\quad+\left(1-\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{{m_{f}}_{2}}\right|}\Bigg\{\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{2}}{w_{1}}\right)}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\times\Big(\Theta({m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2})\Big[\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)-t^{2}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1}-1,{m_{f}}_{2}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\Big]\\
&\quad\qquad\,\,\ +\Theta({m_{f}}_{2}-{m_{f}}_{1}-1)\Big[(m_{f_{1}}\leftrightarrow m_{f_{2}})+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\Big]\Big)\\
&\quad+\left(1-\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\right)t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}\sum_{i\neq j=1}^{2}\left[w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{i}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{i}\right)\right]\left[w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{j}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{j}\right)\right]\Bigg\}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.72)

where we denote by
\Theta(x)
the Heaviside step function, such that
\Theta(x)=1
for
x\geq 0
and
\Theta(x)=0
for
x<0
. The expressions for the
\U(1)
and
\U(2)
Hall-Littlewood polynomials are given by (see
(
19
, (B.13))
)

\begin{split}\Psi^{(n)}_{\U(1)}\left(x\right)&=x^{n}~,\\
\Psi^{(n_{1},n_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t;x_{1},x_{2}\right)&=\frac{tx_{1}^{1+n_{2}}x_{2}^{n_{1}}-x_{1}^{n_{2}}x_{2}^{1+n_{1}}+x_{1}^{1+n_{1}}x_{2}^{n_{2}}-tx_{1}^{n_{1}}x_{2}^{1+n_{2}}}{x_{1}-x_{2}}~.\end{split}

(3.73)

Limit of the index of quivers with
T[\U(2)]
building block

We are now ready to discuss the NS5-limit of quiver (
3.62
), where the axial fugacity assignments are summarised in Table (
3.68
). This can be obtained starting from the expression

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2^{4}}\oint\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{i})^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}w_{i}^{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\times\Bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{4}Z_{\text{vec}}^{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z};t;\{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}\}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{L}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(t;\mathfrak{z};(f^{(i)}_{L})^{-s}(u^{(\alpha)}_{1})^{s};sm^{(\alpha)}_{1}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(\mathfrak{z};t;(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s}(u^{(\beta)}_{3})^{-s};sm^{(\alpha)}_{2}-sm^{(\beta)}_{3}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{R}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(t;\mathfrak{z};(f^{i}_{R})^{-s}(u^{(\alpha)}_{4})^{s};sm^{(\alpha)}_{4}\right)\Bigg]_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}\\
&\qquad\times Z^{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z};t|\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}|\{u^{(\alpha)}_{2}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\})\\
&\qquad\times Z^{t\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(t;\mathfrak{z}|\{(u^{(\alpha)}_{3})^{-1}\};\{-m^{(\alpha)}_{3}\}|\{u^{(\alpha)}_{4}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{4}\})~,\end{split}

(3.74)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{\text{vec}}^{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z};t;\{u^{(\alpha)}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}\}\right)&=\left(\mathfrak{z}t\right)^{-\left|m^{(1)}-m^{(2)}\right|}+(-1)^{2m^{(1)}-2m^{(2)}}\left(\mathfrak{z}t\right)^{\left|m^{(1)}-m^{(2)}\right|}\\
&-(-1)^{m^{(1)}-m^{(2)}}\left[\frac{u^{(1)}}{u^{(2)}}+\frac{u^{(2)}}{u^{(1)}}\right]~,\end{split}

(3.75a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(\mathfrak{z};t;u;m_{u}\right)&=\left(\mathfrak{z}u^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m_{u}\right|}{2}}\\
&\times\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1-(-1)^{m_{u}}\mathfrak{z}^{2+\left|m_{u}\right|+2j}t^{1+\left|m_{u}\right|+2j}u^{-1}}{1-(-1)^{m_{u}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|m_{u}\right|+2j}t^{1+\left|m_{u}\right|+2j}u}~.\end{split}

(3.75b)

Observe that the lowest possible power of
\mathfrak{z}
in (
3.74
) is

\begin{split}F(\mathfrak{z})=&-\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|-\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|\\
&+\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(1)}_{3}\right|+\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|+\left|m^{(2)}_{2}-m^{(1)}_{3}\right|+\left|m^{(2)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|~,\end{split}

(3.76)

where, thanks to the triangle inequality, it follows that
F(\mathfrak{z})\geq 0
for all values of the magnetic fluxes,
18
18

18

Observe that, in the case
\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|\geq\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|
, we can use the inequality
\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(i)}_{3}\right|+\left|m^{(2)}_{2}-m^{(i)}_{3}\right|\geq\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|
for
i=\{1,2\}
, which leads to
F(\mathfrak{z})\geq\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|-\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|\geq 0
. Analogously, if
\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|\geq\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|
, the inequality
\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(i)}_{2}\right|+\left|m^{(2)}_{3}-m^{(i)}_{2}\right|\geq\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|
ensures that
F(\mathfrak{z})\geq-\left|m^{(1)}_{2}-m^{(2)}_{2}\right|+\left|m^{(1)}_{3}-m^{(2)}_{3}\right|\geq 0
.
leading to the crucial fact that there is no divergence in taking the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit. Moreover, if
F(\mathfrak{z})>0
, the expression (
3.74
) vanishes in the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit, meaning that the relevant contribution is the one coming from the choice of magnetic fluxes satisfying
F(\mathfrak{z})=0
. This can happen in three different ways:

•

when all magnetic fluxes associated with the second and third
\U(2)
gauge nodes in (
3.61
) are equal,

•

when
\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3}\}
,

•

when
\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}\}
.

Let us discuss the various possible cases separately, using the fact that
Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(\mathfrak{z};t;u;m_{u}\right)
in the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit reduces to
\left(\mathfrak{z}u^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m_{u}\right|}{2}}
if
m_{u}\neq 0
, or to
\mathop{\rm PE}\left[tu\right]
if
m_{u}=0
.

1.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}\}
: the only non-trivial contributions come from the sectors with
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}\}
, for which the expression (
3.74
) simplifies to

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2^{4}}\sum_{\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}}\oint\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\right)w_{1}^{2\mathfrak{l}}\left(w_{2}w_{3}\right)^{2\mathfrak{m}}w_{4}^{2\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(u^{(1)}_{1}u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}}\left(u^{(1)}_{2}u^{(2)}_{2}\right)^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{m}}\left(u^{(1)}_{3}u^{(2)}_{3}\right)^{-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{m}}\left(u^{(1)}_{4}u^{(2)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\qquad\times\left[\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(2-\frac{u^{(1)}_{i}}{u^{(2)}_{i}}-\frac{u^{(2)}_{i}}{u^{(1)}_{i}}\right)\right]\times t^{2L\left|\mathfrak{l}\right|+2R\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}\\
&\qquad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}t(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s}(u^{(\beta)}_{3})^{-s}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\left(u^{(1)}_{1}u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}}\left(u^{(1)}_{2}u^{(2)}_{2}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}}\times\frac{1+t^{2}}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{u^{(1)}_{1}}{u^{(2)}_{1}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{u^{(2)}_{1}}{u^{(1)}_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad\times\left(u^{(1)}_{3}u^{(2)}_{3}\right)^{-\mathfrak{n}}\left(u^{(1)}_{4}u^{(2)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{m}}\times\frac{1+t^{2}}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{u^{(1)}_{4}}{u^{(2)}_{4}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{u^{(2)}_{4}}{u^{(1)}_{4}}\right)}~,\end{split}

(3.77)

whre the Chern-Simons levels are parametrised as in (
3.64
). The integration over the variables
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
and
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{4}\}
yields a non-zero result if
\mathfrak{m}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}
, with
\mathfrak{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Such a condition on the magnetic fluxes reduces the expression above into a single summation, for instance by expressing
\mathfrak{l}
and
\mathfrak{n}
as functions of
\mathfrak{m}
. Upon introducing the variable
w=w_{1}^{-1}(w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{q}}w_{4}^{-1}
, the computation of the residues at the poles of the gauge fugacities finally results in

\begin{split}&\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathfrak{q}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{w^{\frac{2\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{q}}}t^{\frac{2\left(-1+2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+L+R\right)\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|}{\mathfrak{q}}}}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)\left(1-t^{4}\right)}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{w^{2m}t^{2{\cal F}\left|m\right|}}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)\left(1-t^{4}\right)}~,\end{split}

(3.78)

where we set
m=\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{q}}
, and define

\begin{split}{\cal F}=L+R+2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1~.\end{split}

(3.79)

We will demonstrate in Appendix
B.3
that the contributions from the magnetic fluxes satisfying
m^{(1)}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{1}
and
m^{(1)}_{4}\neq m^{(2)}_{4}
vanish due to the residue theorem.

2.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{1},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{2}\}
: the only non-vanishing contributions come from the sectors with
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}_{2}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}_{2}\}
, for which the expression (
3.74
) becomes

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2^{4}}\sum_{\mathfrak{l}_{1},\mathfrak{l}_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2},\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\oint\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\right)w_{1}^{\mathfrak{l}_{1}+\mathfrak{l}_{2}}\left(w_{2}w_{3}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}}w_{4}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}+\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(u^{(1)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{2}}\left(\frac{u^{(1)}_{2}}{u^{(1)}_{3}}\right)^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\left(\frac{u^{(2)}_{2}}{u^{(2)}_{3}}\right)^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\left(u^{(1)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\left[\left(\mathfrak{z}t\right)^{-\left(\left|\mathfrak{l}_{1}-\mathfrak{l}_{2}\right|+2\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{n}_{1}-\mathfrak{n}_{2}\right|\right)}\right]\times t^{L\left(\left|\mathfrak{l}_{1}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{l}_{2}\right|\right)+R\left(\left|\mathfrak{n}_{1}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{n}_{2}\right|\right)}\\
&\qquad\times\mathfrak{z}^{2\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}t(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s}(u^{(\alpha)}_{3})^{-s}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\mathfrak{z}^{\left|\mathfrak{l}_{1}-\mathfrak{l}_{2}\right|}t^{\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|}\Big[\left(u^{(1)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\left(u^{(1)}_{2}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{2}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\ +\left(u^{(1)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\left(u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\left(u^{(1)}_{2}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}_{2}}\left(u^{(2)}_{2}\right)^{\mathfrak{l}_{1}}\Big]\\
&\qquad\times\mathfrak{z}^{\left|\mathfrak{n}_{1}-\mathfrak{n}_{2}\right|}t^{\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|}\Big[\left(u^{(1)}_{3}\right)^{-\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{3}\right)^{-\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\left(u^{(1)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\left(u^{(2)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\ +\left(u^{(1)}_{3}\right)^{-\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\left(u^{(2)}_{3}\right)^{-\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\left(u^{(1)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\left(u^{(2)}_{4}\right)^{-\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\Big]~.\end{split}

(3.80)

Upon performing the integration over the gauge fugacities
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
and
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{4}\}
, these pick up residues at the poles for the following four choices of magnetic fluxes:
\{\mathfrak{m}_{1}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{2}\lor\mathfrak{m}_{1}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}_{1}\}
and
\{\mathfrak{m}_{1}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{2}\lor\mathfrak{m}_{1}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2}=-\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{n}_{1}\}
. These conditions simplify the integral above, which can now be expressed just as a sum over two magnetic fluxes, say
\mathfrak{m}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{2}
, as follows:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{2}\in\mathfrak{q}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{w^{\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{1}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}}{\mathfrak{q}}}t^{\frac{-2\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|+\left(-1+L+R+2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}\right)\left(\left|\mathfrak{m}_{1}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{m}_{2}\right|\right)}{\mathfrak{q}}}}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)^{2}}\\
=&\frac{1}{4}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{w^{m_{1}+m_{2}}t^{-2\left|m_{1}-m_{2}\right|+{\cal F}\left(\left|m_{1}\right|+\left|m_{2}\right|\right)}}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)^{2}}~,\end{split}

(3.81)

where we set
w=w_{1}^{-1}(w_{2}w_{3})^{\mathfrak{q}}w_{4}^{-1}
and, in the second equality, we define
m_{\alpha}=\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{\alpha}}{\mathfrak{q}}
for
\alpha=1,2
and use (
3.79
).

On the other hand, we will demonstrate in Appendix
B.3
that the magnetic fluxes satisfying
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}\}
do not contribute to the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index.

3.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{1},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{2}\}
: this choice of magnetic fluxes is analogous to the previous case, to which is related simply by permuting
m^{(1)}_{3}
and
m^{(2)}_{3}
. Upon setting the topological fugacities of the theory to one, the action of the
\U(2)
Weyl group ensures that the limit of the index (
3.74
) in this case is the same as (
3.81
).

We can finally consider the total contribution coming from (
3.78
) and twice (
3.81
). Taking into account the action of the
\U(2)
Weyl group, the sum in the latter expression can be reformulated by considering an ordered set of magnetic fluxes satisfying
m_{1}\geq m_{2}
, and thus multiplying by an overall factor of two. If we further use the definition of the
\U(2)
dressing factor given in (
A.3
), the resulting total contribution can then be expressed as

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.98}{$\begin{split}&\eqref{limitTU2ma}+2\eqref{limitTU2m1m2a}\\
&=\sum_{m_{1}\geq m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}P_{\U(2)}(t;m_{1},m_{2})w^{m_{1}+m_{2}}t^{-2\left|m_{1}-m_{2}\right|+{\cal F}\left(\left|m_{1}\right|+\left|m_{2}\right|\right)}\\
&=\frac{\left(1-t^{2{\cal F}-2}\right)\left(1-t^{2{\cal F}}\right)}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)\left(1-t^{4}\right)\left(1-w^{\pm}t^{{\cal F}-2}\right)\left(1-w^{\pm}t^{{\cal F}}\right)}\\
&=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t^{4}+\left(w+\frac{1}{w}\right)t^{{\cal F}-2}+\left(w+\frac{1}{w}\right)t^{{\cal F}}-t^{2\left({\cal F}-1\right)}-t^{2{\cal F}}\right]~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.82)

where
1-w^{\pm}t
stands for
\left(1-wt\right)\left(1-w^{-1}t\right)
. This expression matches the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of
{\cal N}=4

\U(2)
SQCD with
{\cal F}=L+R+2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1
flavours. This result finds agreement with the magnetic quiver prediction for the NS5-branch reported in
(
50
, (5.15))
.
19
19

19

Note that the authors of that reference consider a slightly different brane setup with respect to the one depicted in (
3.60
).

As we can read from (
3.82
), there are six generators subject to two relations, which can be mapped to those described for 3d
{\cal N}=4

\U(2)
SQCD in
(
21
, around (5.4))
as follows.
20
20

20

The case we are interested in corresponds to setting
k=2
and
n=L+R+2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1
in the notation used in that reference, where
{\cal N}=4

\U(k)
SQCD with
n
flavours is considered.
The generators of the Coulomb branch of
\U(2)
SQCD can be built from the adjoint field
\Phi
in the
{\cal N}=4
vector multiplet, which can be put in a diagonal form as
\Phi=\mathrm{diag}(\psi,\phi)
, and the monopole operator with
\U(2)
magnetic fluxes
\pm(1,0)
, denoted
V_{\pm(1,0)}
, as detailed in the aforementioned reference. We tabulate such generators and the corresponding gauge invariant quantities in theory (
3.61
) below.

\begin{split}\begin{tabular}[]{c|c}\hline\cr Generators of $\U(2)$ SQCD&Corresponding operators in (\ref {quivpqbraneTU2})\\
\hline\cr$\mathop{\rm tr}(\Phi)$&$\mathop{\rm tr}(Y_{0})$\\
$\mathop{\rm tr}(\Phi^{2})$&$\mathop{\rm tr}(Y_{0}^{2})$\\
$V_{(1,0)}$&$Y_{+}\left[(X_{23})^{1}_{1}\right]^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}$\\
$V_{(-1,0)}$&$Y_{-}\left[(X_{32})^{1}_{1}\right]^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}$\\
$V_{(1,0)}\phi$&$Y_{+}\left[(X_{23})^{1}_{1}\right]^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}y$\\
$V_{(-1,0)}\phi$&$Y_{-}\left[(X_{32})^{1}_{1}\right]^{2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}-1}y$\\

(3.83)

where we define
Y_{0}=X_{23}X_{32}
, which can be put in a diagonal form as
Y_{0}=\mathrm{diag}(x,y)
, and the monopole operators
Y_{\pm}
are those with fluxes
\pm(1,0;-\mathfrak{q},0;-\mathfrak{q},0;1,0)
under the four
\U(2)
gauge groups. Note that the gauge charge of such bare monopole operators can be read off from the second, fifth and seventh lines of (
3.80
), namely

(u_{1}^{(1)})^{\mathfrak{q}}(u_{2}^{(1)}/u_{3}^{(1)})^{-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}}(u_{4}^{(1)})^{-\mathfrak{q}}\times(u_{1}^{(1)})^{-\mathfrak{q}}(u_{2}^{(1)})\times(u_{3}^{(1)})^{-1}(u_{4}^{(1)})^{\mathfrak{q}}=(u_{2}^{(1)}/u_{3}^{(1)})^{-2\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{q}+1}
.
21
21

21

In particular, the terms
t^{2}
and
t^{4}
appearing in the plethystic exponential in (
3.82
) correspond to the generators
\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}}
and
\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{2}}
, respectively. Note that the operators
\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{m+2}}
, with

\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{m+2}}=\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}}\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{m+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{2}}\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{m}}-(\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}})^{2}\mathop{\rm tr}{Y_{0}^{m}}\right]
.

(3.85)

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.96}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivT4axial}}=\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{7}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{7}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{i}^{\kappa_{i}m_{i}}w_{i}^{m_{i}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i};0\right)\Bigg)\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{6}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{i}(u_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s};sm_{i}-sm_{i+1}\right)\prod_{j=4,6,7}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{j}(f^{-1}_{j}u_{j})^{s};sm_{j}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(3.88)

and, for each branch, the fugacities

,

and

\begin{split}&\begin{tabular}[]{c|ccccccccc}\hline\cr Branch&$\mathfrak{a}_{1}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{2}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{3}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{4}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{5}$&$\mathfrak{a}_{6}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{4}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{6}$&$\mathfrak{c}_{7}$\\
