\hline\cr\end{tabular}\end{split}

(4.30)

The index for the theory in (
4.29
) is then given by:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.92}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{m_{1},m_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{2\kappa_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\Bigg)\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}(u_{1})^{2\kappa_{1}m_{1}}w_{1}^{m_{1}}\\
&\quad\times\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}w_{3}^{m_{3}}\,\,Z_{\text{vec}}^{\mathrm{\USp}(4)}(x;\{u^{(\alpha)}_{2}\};\{m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\})\,\,Z_{\text{adj-chir}}\,\,Z_{\text{matter}}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.31)

where the Chern-Simons levels are

\begin{split}\kappa_{1}=k~,\qquad\kappa_{2}=-k~,\end{split}

(4.32)

and we define

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{$\begin{split}Z_{\text{adj-chir}}&=\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\prod_{\beta>\alpha}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{2}(u^{\alpha}_{2})^{s_{1}}(u^{\beta}_{2})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}+s_{2}m^{(\beta)}_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{2}(u^{\alpha}_{2})^{2s};2sm^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{2};0\right)^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{1};0\right)Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{3};0\right)~,\\
Z_{\text{matter}}&=\Bigg[\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{1}(b_{1}u_{1})^{s_{1}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad\quad\times Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{2}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{1}}(b_{2}u_{3})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}+s_{2}m_{3}\right)\Bigg]\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{2}=1}^{F_{2}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{2}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{1}}(f^{(\rho_{2})}_{2})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{1}=1}^{F_{1}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{1}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(f^{(\rho_{1})}_{1})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m_{1}\right)(1\leftrightarrow 3)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.33)

Note that
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are introduced for convenience in keeping track of bifundamental hypermultiplets. They do not correspond to a symmetry of the theory and can be set to unity. Moreover, the refinement with respect to the charge conjugation symmetry fugacities
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
for gauge groups
\mathrm{SO}(2)_{2k}
and
\mathrm{SO}(2)
in (
4.29
) can be done in a similar fashion to (
4.5
).

For definiteness, we take
k>0
throughout this discussion. The Hilbert series for each branch is obtained by taking a specific limit of the index:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}[\text{branch}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\};\{f_{i}\})=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}\Big|_{\eqref{branchpuzzle1},\,\eqref{N=3reparam}}~.\end{split}

(4.34)

To compute the limit, we first examine the overall power of
\mathfrak{z}
in the integrand for each branch. A non-vanishing result in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit sets the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
to zero, imposing the following constraints on the magnetic fluxes
m_{i}
:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.85}{
\begin{tabular}[]{c|l|l}\hline\cr Branch&Exponent of $\mathfrak{z}$&Condition\\
\hline\cr NS5&${\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|m_{1}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|-2|m_{2}^{(i)}|\right)-|m_{2}^{(1)}\pm m_{2}^{(2)}|}$&$m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(i)}~,\,\,m_{2}^{(j\neq i)}=0$\\
D5&${\sum_{i=1}^{2}(|m_{1}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|+|m_{3}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|)}$&$m_{1}=m_{2}^{(1)}=m_{2}^{(2)}=m_{3}=0$\\
&${+2F_{1}|m_{1}|+2F_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}(|m_{2}^{(1)}|)+2F_{3}|m_{3}|}$&\\
&$-2|m_{2}^{(1)}\pm m_{2}^{(2)}|-4\sum_{i=1}^{2}|m_{2}^{(i)}|$&\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular} }\end{split}

(4.35)

where
|m_{i}\pm m_{j}|=|m_{i}+m_{j}|+|m_{i}-m_{j}|
, and, in the first case, there is no restriction on
m_{3}
. Notice that the conditions in Table
4.35
are not the only ones that can be obtained searching for solutions of the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
equal to zero.
35
35

35

The same argument as in Footnote
22
applies here.
,
36
36

36

In this case, another subtlety arises. There are values of the fluxes for which the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
is negative. We conjecture that the integral has no residues for these values of the fluxes. This can be easily checked expanding the index up to low order and seeing that it gives zero for values of the fluxes different from the one considered. This is because monopole operators corresponding to those fluxes cannot be dressed properly and so cannot be made gauge invariant.

We now discuss the computation for each branch explicitly.

The NS5-branch

The condition obtained in Table (
4.35
) for the NS5-branch limit of the index is

\begin{split}m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(i)}~,\,\,\,\,m_{2}^{(j\neq i)}=0~,\quad i,j=1,2~.\end{split}

(4.36)

Using the Weyl symmetry of the
\USp(4)
gauge group, we can restrict our attention to the option
m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(1)},m_{2}^{(2)}=0
. Taking the limit (
4.34
), the Hilbert series receives the following contributions: one with
m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\pm\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
, where
\mathfrak{m},\neq 0
, and the other with
m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
. In the first case, the signs
+
and
-
contribute equally to the limit of the index, so

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})=2\times H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}+H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(4.37)

For the fluxes
m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
, the
\USp(4)
gauge group is broken to
\text{SU(2)}\times\text{U(1)}
. In this case, when both charge conjugation fugacities
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are set to one, the limit of the index is
37
37

37

Note that we have an overall factor of
\frac{1}{4}
here, in contrast to that of (
4.31
), which is
\frac{1}{8}
. This is because the results for the allowed choices
\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0)
and
(0,\mathfrak{m})
are equal.

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})}\\
&\quad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}u_{1}^{2\kappa_{1}\mathfrak{m}}w_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{2\kappa_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\Bigg)_{\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0)}\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{2}^{(2)}\right)^{2s}\right]\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\left(\frac{b_{1}u_{1}}{u^{(1)}_{2}}\right)^{s}\right]~,\end{split}

(4.38)

where the dimension of the monopole operators is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}&\Delta(m_{1},m_{2}^{(1)},m_{2}^{(2)},m_{3})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s=\pm 1}\bigl(|m_{3}+sm_{2}^{(i)}|+|m_{1}+sm_{2}^{(i)}|\bigr)-2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigl(|2m_{2}^{(i)}|\bigr)\\
&\qquad-2|m_{2}^{(1)}-m_{2}^{(2)}|-2|m_{2}^{(1)}+m_{2}^{(2)}|+2F_{1}|m_{1}|+2F_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|m_{2}^{(i)}|+2F_{3}|m_{3}|\Biggr).\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.39)

Explicitly, for this set of magnetic fluxes, the monopole dimension reads

\begin{split}&2\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})\\
&=|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}|(2F_{1}+2F_{2}-4)+|\mathfrak{n}|(2F_{3}+2)~.\end{split}

(4.40)

Similarly to (
A.1
) and (
A.2
), the contribution (
4.38
) gets simplified to:

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{2k|\mathfrak{m}|}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}(b_{1}^{-2k}w_{1})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}\times t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k-2)|\mathfrak{m}|+2(F_{3}+1)|\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}(b_{1}^{-2k}w_{1})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(4.41)

The analogous expression with
\chi_{2}=-1
can be obtained from (
4.38
) by setting
\mathfrak{n}=0
, replacing the contribution
\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}]=\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}
due to the adjoint chiral multiplet with
\frac{1}{PE_{F}[t^{2}]}=\frac{1}{1+t^{2}}
, and multiplying by an overall phase factor
\left(-1\right)^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|}
arising from the chiral fields with axial fugacity
\mathfrak{a}_{2}=a^{-1}
connecting the
\USp(4)_{k}
gauge node with the
\mathrm{SO}(2)
gauge node in (
4.29
). Observe, instead, that
\chi_{1}
is always equal to one when this set of magnetic fluxes is considered, since the
\mathrm{SO}(2)_{-2k}
flux
\mathfrak{m}
is different from zero. Explicitly, the result refined with respect to the charge conjugation symmetry fugacities
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
for gauge groups
\mathrm{SO}(2)_{2k}
and
\mathrm{SO}(2)
in (
4.27
), respectively, is

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}\chi_{2}^{\mathfrak{m}}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{1}=+1)\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{2})(b_{1}^{-2k}w_{1})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\qquad\quad\quad\times t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k-2)|\mathfrak{m}|+2(F_{3}+1)|\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|}\Big|_{\text{$\mathfrak{n}=0$ if $\chi_{2}=-1$}}~,\end{split}

(4.42)

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(t;\chi)=\begin{cases}P_{\mathrm{SO}(2)}(t)=\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}]=(1-t^{2})^{-1}~,&\quad\chi=1\\
2P_{\USp(2)}(t)-P_{\mathrm{SO}(2)}(t)=\frac{1}{\mathop{\rm PE}_{F}[t^{2}]}=(1+t^{2})^{-1}&\quad\chi=-1\\
\end{cases}~,\end{split}

(4.43)

defined as in (
4.17
) and (
4.18
). We emphasise that, in the expression (
4.42
),
\chi_{1}
is always equal to
+1
and, when
\chi_{2}=-1
, we have to set the flux
\mathfrak{n}=0
.

For
m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
, the
\USp(4)
gauge group is unbroken and the corresponding contribution to the limit of the index with
\chi_{1}=\chi_{2}=+1
reads

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=0,\ \bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})}\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}\\
&\,\,\times\oint\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{2}^{(i)}\right)^{2s}\right]\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{2}^{(1)}\right)^{s_{1}}\left(u_{2}^{(2)}\right)^{s_{2}}\right]\\
&\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t\sum_{s1,s2=\pm 1}\left[\left(b_{1}^{s_{1}}u_{1}^{s_{1}}(u^{(i)}_{2})^{s_{2}}\right)\right]\right]~,\end{split}

(4.44)

where, in this case, we have

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1}=0,\ \bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})=(2F_{3}+4)|\mathfrak{n}|~.\end{split}

(4.45)

When
\chi_{2}=-1
, the expression (
4.44
) gets modified analogously to the discussion below (

): we have to set the flux
\mathfrak{n}=0
and replace
\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}]=\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}
with
\mathop{\rm PE}_{F}[t^{2}]=\frac{1}{1+t^{2}}
, as in (

). On the other hand, when
\chi_{1}=-1
, we have to set

and

in the contribution due to the plethystic exponential in the last line of (

), which then contains the terms
\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left(1-t(u^{(i)}_{2})^{s}\right)^{-1}\left(1+t(u^{(i)}_{2})^{s}\right)^{-1}
. As above, we obtain the following result refined with the charge conjugation fugacities
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
:

\begin{split}H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2(F_{3}+2)|\mathfrak{n}|}\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{1})\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{2})w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}~,\end{split}

(4.46)

where
\mathcal{D}(t;\chi)
is given by (
4.43
). We remark that, when
\chi_{2}=-1
, we have to set the flux
\mathfrak{n}=0
.

Summing the three contributions as indicated in (
4.37
), we obtain

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\},\{b_{i}\})\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k-2)|\mathfrak{m}|+2(F_{3}+1)|\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|}\\
&\qquad\quad\quad\times\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{1})\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{2})\chi_{2}^{\mathfrak{m}}(b_{1}^{-2k}w_{1})^{\mathfrak{m}}w_{3}^{\mathfrak{n}}\Big|_{\text{$(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})=0$ if $(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=-1$}}~,\end{split}

(4.47)

which is the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of the following quiver:
38
38

38

Here
(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})
,
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
are the quantities associated with the left and right
\mathrm{SO}(2)
nodes, respectively. Note that only the phase
\chi_{2}^{\mathfrak{m}}
(but not

) appears. The explanation is due to the fact that, as summarised in (

), the magnetic flux

associated with the rightmost

) is unconstrained, hence, when

and, consequently,

, a non-trivial phase factor

can arise in the

limit of the index due to the contribution associated with the chiral fields with axial fugacity

in (

). On the other hand, the magnetic flux

associated with the leftmost

) is forced to be equal to one of the two
\USp(4)

m^{(1)}_{2}
, with the other flux

). Hence, upon taking the

and, consequently,

in (

) only give rise to trivial phases

and

.

\mathrm{SO}(2)

(4.48)

Note that the half-hypermultiplet in the representation
[\mathbf{2};\mathbf{2}]
of

Further comments on the magnetic quiver.

Following the appearance of this paper on the arXiv, Ref.
(
49
, v2)
was released. The authors of that reference moved the discussion regarding theory (
4.27
) to Section 4.2.2, where they consider the special case
F_{1}=F_{2}=F_{3}=0
. Consequently, they no longer provide (
4.48
) as a magnetic quiver for the NS5-branch of (
4.27
). Their revised magnetic quiver candidate is instead given by
(
49
, v2, (4.22))
, which, for generic
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
, and
F_{3}
, reads

\USp(2)

\mathrm{SO}(2)
k+F_{1}+F_{2}
F_{3}+1

(4.49)

whose Coulomb branch Hilbert series can be obtained from (
4.47
) by restricting the summation to run over
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and by replacing
\mathcal{D}(t;\chi_{1})
with
P_{\USp(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m})
. As observed from (
4.43
), this modification is exactly equivalent to summing the fugacity
\chi_{1}
over
\pm 1
and dividing by two in (
4.47
), see also the discussion around (
4.20
). As a result, we conclude that (
4.49
) is a magnetic quiver capturing the NS5-branch of the variant of theory (
4.27
) in which the charge conjugation symmetry associated with the
\mathrm{SO}(2)_{2k}
node is gauged, thus turning it into an
\mathrm{O}(2)_{2k}
node.

A magnetic quiver describing the NS5-branch of theory (
4.27
), where the leftmost gauge node is
truly

\mathrm{SO}(2)_{2k}
, can also be realised starting from (
4.49
) by turning on the background flux
\bm{\nu}_{b}=(b,0,\ldots,0)
, with
b\in\{0,1\}
, for the
\mathrm{SO}(2k+2F_{1}+2F_{2})
flavour node. Denoting the magnetic and charge conjugation fugacities associated with the
\mathrm{SO}(2k+2F_{1}+2F_{2})
flavour node of (
4.49
) by
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}
, respectively, the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of (
4.49
) summed over
b\in\{0,1\}
equals the NS5-branch limit (
4.47
), provided the identification
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}=\chi_{1}
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}=\zeta_{1}\chi_{1}
holds, see
(
49
, v2, (4.23))
. Specifically, the contribution due to
\bm{\nu}_{1}
to the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of (
4.49
) coincides with the terms in the NS5-branch limit of the index of (
4.27
) that are projected out upon gauging the charge conjugation symmetry with fugacity
\chi_{1}
. This implies the following equality:

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{newmquiverNSpuzzle1}}[\text{Coulomb}](\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}=\chi_{1},\widetilde{\chi}_{1}=\zeta_{1}\chi_{1})\Big|_{\bm{\nu}_{b}=\bm{\nu}_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)}\\
&=(\ref {HSNS5branchpuzzle1})_{\chi_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}\Big[(\ref {HSNS5branchpuzzle1})_{\chi_{1}=1}+(\ref {HSNS5branchpuzzle1})_{\chi_{1}=-1}\Big]_{\zeta_{1}\rightarrow\zeta_{1}\chi_{1}}~,\end{split}

(4.50)

where we point out that
\zeta_{1}
must be redefined as
\zeta_{1}\chi_{1}
in the contribution appearing inside the square brackets on the second line of the above identity.
39
39

39

Recall that, without turning on any background flux for the flavour symmetry, (
4.49
) is a magnetic quiver for the NS5-branch of theory (
4.27
) with the
\mathrm{SO}(2)_{2k}
gauge node replaced by
\mathrm{O}(2)_{2k}
, whose Hilbert series is given by the quantity appearing inside the square brackets in (
4.50
), divided by two. Note that this quantity does not depend on
\chi_{1}
, since the corresponding charge conjugation symmetry has been gauged. On the other hand, the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of (
4.49
) refined with
\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}
contains a term
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}=\zeta_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi_{1}^{\mathfrak{m}}
, even if
\bm{\nu}_{1}
is turned off. The redefinition
\zeta_{1}\rightarrow\zeta_{1}\chi_{1}
is thus necessary to match the two Hilbert series.
The validity of (
4.50
) can be checked for generic values of the fugacities
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{2}
,
\chi_{1}
, and
\chi_{2}
by expanding both sides up to a sufficiently high order in
t
.
40
40

40

Actually, for the case
\chi_{2}=-1
, the validity of (
4.50
) can be easily shown analytically using the identity

\begin{split}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k-2)|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{m}+1|+|\mathfrak{m}-1|}=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}t^{2(F_{1}+F_{2}+k-1)|\mathfrak{m}|}~.\end{split}

(4.51)

The D5-branch

Finally, the Hilbert series for the D5-branch is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle1}}[\text{D5}](t;b_{1,2})=\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{8}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du_{2}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{2}^{(i)}}\right)\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\\
&\,\,\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left(1-(u_{2}^{(i)})^{2s}\right)\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left(1-(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}\right)\\
&\,\,\times\mathop{\rm PE}\Bigg[t\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{2}b_{1}^{s_{1}}u_{1}^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{2}}+b_{2}^{s_{2}}u_{3}^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{2}}\right\}\\
&\,\,\,\qquad+t\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\Big\{\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{1}}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(f_{1}^{(\rho_{1})})^{s_{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{2}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{1}}(f_{2}^{(\rho_{2})})^{s_{2}}+\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{3}}(u_{3})^{s_{1}}(f_{3}^{(\rho_{3})})^{s_{2}}\Big\}\\
&\,\,\,\qquad-2t^{2}-t^{2}\Bigg\{\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s=\pm 1}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{2s}+2\Bigg\}\Bigg]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.52)

The coefficient of
-t^{2}
inside the plethystic exponential accounts for the adjoint chiral multiplets associated with the three gauge nodes, which have an axial fugacity of
a^{-2}
. This result corresponds to the Higgs branch Hilbert series of the theory in (
4.29
) with the Chern-Simons levels set to zero (
k=0
), which is the 3d
{\cal N}=4
theory

\mathrm{SO}(2)

\mathrm{\USp}(4)

\mathrm{SO}(2)
F_{1}
F_{2}
F_{3}

(4.53)

The refinement with respect to the charge conjugation symmetry fugacities is straightforward.

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.92}{$\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}&=\frac{1}{32}\sum_{m_{1},m_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\{m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}u_{1}^{2\kappa_{1}m_{1}}\Bigg(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{2\kappa_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\Bigg)\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\\

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&Z_{\text{adj-chir}}=\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq 2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{2}(u^{\alpha}_{2})^{s_{1}}(u^{\beta}_{2})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}+s_{2}m^{(\beta)}_{2}\right)\\

(4.61)

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&Z_{\text{matter}}=\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{1}u_{1}^{s_{1}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{1}\chi u_{1}^{s};sm_{1}\right)\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{2}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{1}}u_{3}^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}+s_{2}m_{3}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{a}_{2}\chi u_{3}^{s};sm_{3}\right)\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{2}=1}^{F_{2}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{2}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{s_{1}}(f_{2}^{\rho_{2}})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{2}=1}^{F_{2}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{2}\chi(f_{2}^{\rho_{2}})^{s};0\right)\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{1}=1}^{F_{1}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{1}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(f_{1}^{(\rho_{1})})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m_{1}\right)\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{1}=\pm 1}\prod_{s_{2}=\pm 1}\prod_{\rho_{3}=1}^{F_{3}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;\mathfrak{c}_{3}(u_{3})^{s_{1}}(f_{3}^{(\rho_{3})})^{s_{2}};s_{1}m_{3}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.62)

with

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}Z_{\text{vec}}^{\mathrm{SO}(5)}(x;\bm{z};\bm{m};\chi)&=x^{-|m_{1}|-|m_{2}|-|m_{1}-m_{2}|-|m_{1}+m_{2}|}\prod_{j=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left(1-(-1)^{sm_{j}}x^{|m_{j}|}z_{j}^{s}\chi\right)\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left(1-(-1)^{s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}}x^{|s_{1}m_{1}+s_{2}m_{2}|}z_{1}^{s_{1}}z_{2}^{s_{2}}\right)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.63)

The Hilbert series for each branch is obtained as follows:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}[\text{branch}](t;\{w_{i}\},\{b_{i}\},\{f_{i}\})=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{\cal I}_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}\Big|_{\eqref{branchpuzzle2},\,\eqref{N=3reparam}}~.\end{split}

(4.64)

To compute the limit, we consider the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
in the integrand for each branch. A non-vanishing result in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit sets such exponents to zero, and thus imposes the following constraints on the magnetic fluxes
m_{i}
:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{
\begin{tabular}[]{c|l|l}\hline\cr Branch&Exponent of $\mathfrak{z}$&Condition\\
\hline\cr NS&${\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(|m_{1}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|-|m_{2}^{(i)}|\right)-|m_{2}^{(1)}\pm m_{2}^{(2)}|-|m_{1}|}$&$m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(i)}~,\,\,m_{2}^{(j\neq i)}=0$\\
D5&${\sum_{i=1}^{2}(|m_{1}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|+|m_{3}\pm m_{2}^{(i)}|)-2|m_{2}^{(1)}\pm m_{2}^{(2)}|}$&$m_{1}=m_{2}^{(1)}=m_{2}^{(2)}=m_{3}=0$\\
&$-2|m_{3}|-2|m_{1}|+2F_{1}|m_{1}|+2F_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}(|m_{2}^{(1)}|)$&\\
&$+2F_{3}|m_{3}|-3\sum_{i=1}^{2}|m_{2}^{(i)}|$&\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular} }\end{split}

(4.65)

where
|m_{i}\pm m_{j}|=|m_{i}+m_{j}|+|m_{i}-m_{j}|
, and, in the first case, there is no restriction on
m_{3}
.

The NS5-branch

The condition obtained in Table (
4.65
) for the NS branch limit of the index is

\begin{split}m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(i)}~,\quad m_{2}^{(j\neq i)}=0~,\,\,i,j=1,2~.\end{split}

(4.66)

Using the Weyl symmetry of the
\mathrm{SO}(5)
gauge group, we can restrict our attention to the option
m_{1}=\pm m_{2}^{(1)},m_{2}^{(2)}=0
. Taking the limit (
4.64
), the Hilbert series receives contributions from the following sets of fluxes:

1.

m_{1}=0~,\quad\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0)~,\quad m_{3}=0~,

2.

m_{1}=\mathfrak{m}~,\quad\bm{m}_{2}=(\pm\mathfrak{m},0)~,\quad m_{3}=0~,

3.

m_{1}=\mathfrak{m}~,\quad\bm{m}_{2}=(\pm\mathfrak{m},0)~,\quad m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}~,

4.

m_{1}=0~,\quad\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0)~,\quad m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}~,

with
\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}\neq 0
. Note that the contributions from the
+
and
-
signs in the second and third cases are equal, so the total Hilbert series is given by

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\};\{b_{i}\})&=H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=0}+2\times H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=0}\\
&+2\times H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}+H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(4.67)

For the fluxes
m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=0
, all the gauge groups are unbroken. In this case, the limit of the index is

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=0}=\frac{1}{32}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=0,\ \bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),\,m_{3}=0)}\times\\
&\quad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\oint\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{1}\right)^{2s}\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{3}\right)^{2s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left[1-(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s}\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}\left(u_{3}^{2}+1+\frac{1}{u_{3}^{2}}\right)+t\left(\chi\sum_{s=\pm 1}\left(u_{1}\right)^{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(u^{(i)}_{2})^{s_{2}}\right)\right]~,\end{split}

(4.68)

where the dimension of the monopole operators is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}&\Delta(m_{1},m_{2}^{(1)},m_{2}^{(2)},m_{3})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Biggl(-2|m_{1}|-2|m_{3}|+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s=\pm 1}\bigl(|m_{3}+sm_{2}^{(i)}|+|m_{1}+sm_{2}^{(i)}|\bigr)-2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigl(|m_{2}^{(i)}|\bigr)\\
&\qquad\,\,-2|m_{2}^{(1)}-m_{2}^{(2)}|-2|m_{2}^{(1)}+m_{2}^{(2)}|+2F_{1}|m_{1}|+2F_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|m_{2}^{(i)}|+2F_{3}|m_{3}|\Biggr)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.69)

The contribution gets simplified to

\begin{split}H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=0}=P_{\USp(2)}(t;0)\times\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{4}]=\frac{1}{(1-t^{4})^{2}}~,\end{split}

(4.70)

where
P_{\USp(2)}(t;0)=\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{4}]=\frac{1}{1-t^{4}}
is the contribution of
\mathop{\rm tr}(\phi^{2})
, where
\phi
is the adjoint chiral field in the vector multiplet of the rightmost
\USp(2)
gauge group, and the other
\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{4}]=\frac{1}{1-t^{4}}
factor is the contribution of the gauge invariant quantity
\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{3}\alpha_{4}}\delta_{i_{1}i_{3}}\delta_{i_{2}i_{4}}X^{i_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}}X^{i_{2}}_{\alpha_{2}}X^{i_{3}}_{\alpha_{3}}X^{i_{4}}_{\alpha_{4}}
, where
X^{i}_{\alpha}
is the bifundamental chiral field in
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{k}\times\mathrm{SO}(5)_{-2k}
with
i=1,2,\ldots,5
and
\alpha=1,2
.

For
m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
, the
\mathrm{SO}(5)
and the first
\USp(2)
gauge group are unbroken, while the last
\USp(2)
group is broken to
\U(1)
, and the corresponding limit of the index is

\begin{split}\scalebox{1}{$\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{32}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=0,\ \bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})}\,\,\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}\\
&\quad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\oint\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{1}\right)^{2s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left[1-(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s}\right]\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t\left(\chi\sum_{s=\pm 1}\left(u_{1}\right)^{s}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(u^{(i)}_{2})^{s_{2}}\right)\right]~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.71)

where, in this case, the dimension of the monopole operator simplifies to

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1}=0,\ \bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})=(F_{3}+1)|\mathfrak{n}|~.\end{split}

(4.72)

As above, we have

\begin{split}H_{m_{1}=0,\bm{m}_{2}=(0,0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{n}>0}\frac{t^{2(1+F_{3})|\mathfrak{n}|)}\chi^{\mathfrak{n}}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}~.\end{split}

(4.73)

For
m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=0
, the
\mathrm{SO}(5)
and the first
\USp(2)
gauge group are broken, while the last
\USp(2)
group is unbroken, and the corresponding contribution to the Hilbert series is
41
41

41

Note that we have an overall factor of
\frac{1}{16}
here, in contrast to that of (
4.58
) which is
\frac{1}{32}
. This is because the results for the allowed choices
\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0)
and
(0,\mathfrak{m})
are equal.

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=0}\\
&=\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=0)}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|}\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}u_{1}^{2k_{1}\mathfrak{m}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\\
&\quad\times\oint\Bigg(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{2\kappa_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\Bigg)_{\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0)}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\left(u_{3}\right)^{2s}\right]\,\,\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}\left(u_{3}^{2}+1+\frac{1}{u_{3}^{2}}\right)+t\left(\sum_{s=\pm 1}u_{1}^{s}(u^{(1)}_{2})^{-s}\right)\right]~,\end{split}

(4.74)

where, in this case, we have

\begin{split}\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=0)=(-1+F_{1}+F_{2})|\mathfrak{m}|~.\end{split}

(4.75)

As above, we find

\begin{split}H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=0}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>0}\frac{t^{2(-1+F_{1}+F_{2}+k)|\mathfrak{m}|)}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\mathfrak{m}}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}~.\end{split}

(4.76)

For
m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}
, all gauge groups are broken to subgroups, and the corresponding limit of the index is

\begin{split}&H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{16}\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}}t^{2\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}\\
&\quad\times\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}u_{1}^{2k_{1}\mathfrak{m}}\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{2}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{2}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{2\kappa_{2}m^{(\alpha)}_{2}}\Bigg)_{\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0)}\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left[1-\chi(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s}\right]\,\,\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+t\left(\sum_{s=\pm 1}u_{1}^{s}(u^{(1)}_{2})^{-s}\right)\right]~,\end{split}

(4.77)

where, in this case, the monopole dimension reduces to

\begin{split}&\Delta(m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\ \bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),\,m_{3}=\mathfrak{n})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(-4|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|+|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+2(F_{1}+F_{2})|\mathfrak{m}|+2F_{3}|\mathfrak{n}|)~.\end{split}

(4.78)

As above, we have

\begin{split}H_{m_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},0),m_{3}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}>0}\frac{t^{2\left((-2+F_{1}+F_{2}+k)|\mathfrak{m}|+\frac{1}{2}|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|+\frac{1}{2}|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+F_{3}|\mathfrak{n}|\right)}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}~.\end{split}

(4.79)

Summing the four contributions as indicated in (
4.67
), we obtain

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}[\text{NS5}](t;\{w_{i}\},\{b_{i}\};\zeta,\chi)\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}>0}\frac{t^{2\widehat{\Delta}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>0}\frac{t^{2\widehat{\Delta}(\mathfrak{m},0)}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}\\
&+\sum_{\mathfrak{n}>0}\frac{t^{2\widehat{\Delta}(0,\mathfrak{n})}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}}{(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})}+\frac{1}{(1-t^{4})^{2}}\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}\geq 0}t^{2\widehat{\Delta}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})}\zeta^{\mathfrak{m}}\chi^{\left|\mathfrak{m}\right|+\left|\mathfrak{n}\right|}P_{\USp(2)}(t;\mathfrak{m})P_{\USp(2)}(t;\mathfrak{n})~,\end{split}

(4.80)

with

\begin{split}\widehat{\Delta}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})=(-2+F_{1}+F_{2}+k)|\mathfrak{m}|+{\color[rgb]{1,0.27,0}\frac{1}{2}}|\mathfrak{m}-\mathfrak{n}|+{\color[rgb]{1,0.27,0}\frac{1}{2}}|\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}|+F_{3}|\mathfrak{n}|~,\end{split}

(4.81)

where we highlight in
orange
the presence of factors of
\frac{1}{2}
in the monopole dimension. This turns out to be equal to the Coulomb branch Hilbert series of the following quiver:

\USp(2)

\USp(2k+2F_{1}+2F_{2}-2)
\mathrm{SO}(2F_{3}+3)

(4.85)

The Coulomb branch Hilbert series of this quiver matches that of (

),
45
45

45

Observe that the
\mathrm{SO}(3)
vector multiplet contributes

to the monopole dimension in (

4.81
).
provided that the magnetic and charge conjugation fugacities,
\widetilde{\zeta}
and
\widetilde{\chi}

\widetilde{\zeta}=\zeta\chi
and
\widetilde{\chi}=\chi
.
46
46

46

With these assignments, the fugacities appear in the Coulomb branch Hilbert series as

4.80
).

47
47

47

In
(

\widetilde{\zeta}=\zeta\chi
,

, and

. Since

is an integer and

is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
fugacity, this result remains consistent with (
4.80
).
A key advantage of this description is that the magnetic fugacity associated with
\zeta
becomes manifest in (
4.85
).

The D5-branch

Finally, the Hilbert series for the D5-branch is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.83}{$\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{quivpuzzle2}}[\text{D5}](t;b_{1,2,3})=\frac{1}{32}\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi iu_{1}}\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du_{2}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{2}^{(i)}}\right)\oint\frac{du_{3}}{2\pi iu_{3}}\\
&\,\,\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left(1-\chi(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s}\right)\prod_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left(1-(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}\right)\prod_{s=\pm 1}(1-u_{1}^{2s})(1\leftrightarrow 3)\\
&\,\,\times\mathop{\rm PE}\Bigg[t\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{2}u_{1}^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{2}}+u_{3}^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{2}}\right\}+2t\chi\sum_{s=\pm 1}\left(u_{1}^{s}+u_{3}^{s}\right)+t\chi\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{2}}\sum_{s_{2}=\pm 1}(f_{2}^{(\rho_{2})})^{s_{2}}\\
&\,\,\,\qquad+t\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}\Big\{\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{1}}(u_{1})^{s_{1}}(f_{1}^{(\rho_{1})})^{s_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 3)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{\rho_{j}=1}^{F_{2}}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s_{1}}(f_{2}^{(\rho_{2})})^{s_{2}}\Big\}\\
&\,\,\,\qquad-t^{2}\left(1+u_{1}^{-2}+u_{1}^{2}\right)-(1\leftrightarrow 3)-t^{2}\Bigg(\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}=\pm 1}(u_{2}^{(1)})^{s_{1}}(u_{2}^{(2)})^{s_{2}}+\chi\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{s=\pm 1}(u_{2}^{(i)})^{s}+2\Bigg)\Bigg]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(4.86)

The coefficient of
-t^{2}
inside the plethystic exponential accounts for the adjoint chirals in the vector multiplets of the three gauge nodes, which have an axial fugacity of
a^{-2}
. This result corresponds to the Higgs branch Hilbert series of the theory in (
4.56
) with the Chern-Simons levels set to zero (
k=0
), which is the 3d
{\cal N}=4
theory

5
Geometric branch

In this section, we describe how to generalise the prescription to study the geometric branches of affine-shaped quivers.

5.1
Circular Abelian quivers: affine
A_{N}
quivers

Let us consider the following theory:

1_{k_{1}}
1_{k_{2}}
1_{k_{3}}
1_{k_{4}}
1_{k_{5}}
1_{k_{n}}
f_{1}
f_{2}
f_{3}
f_{4}
f_{5}
f_{n}

(5.1)

where we write

\begin{split}F=f_{1}+f_{2}+\cdots+f_{n}~,\end{split}

(5.2)

and assume that

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}=0~.\end{split}

(5.3)

Let us denote the chiral multiplets in each bifundamental hypermultiplet
{\cal S}_{i}
between node
\U(1)_{k_{i}}
and
\U(1)_{k_{i+1}}
by
(A_{i},B_{i})
, and those in each fundamental hypermultiplet
{\cal Q}_{i}
of the node
\U(1)_{k_{i}}
by
(Q_{i},\widetilde{Q}_{i})
. The superpotential is

\begin{split}W=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{i}(A_{i}B_{i}-B_{i-1}A_{i-1}+Q_{i}\widetilde{Q}_{i})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\phi_{i}^{2}~.\end{split}

(5.4)

This theory was studied in
28
, and the special case in which
f_{i}=0
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
was studied in
42
. Since
\phi_{i}
are massive, we can integrate them out and obtain

\begin{split}W_{\text{eff}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\pi}{k_{i}}(A_{i}B_{i}-B_{i-1}A_{i-1}+Q_{i}\widetilde{Q}_{i})^{2}~.\end{split}

(5.5)

The index of this theory is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{1}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{CSquivflv}}(x;\{w_{i}\};\{v_{i}\};\{y_{i,\ell}\})\\
&=\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi u_{1}}\ldots\frac{du_{n}}{2\pi u_{n}}\,\,\prod_{i=1}u_{i}^{k_{i}m_{i}}w_{i}^{m_{i}}\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;(v_{i}u_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s};s(m_{i}-m_{i+1})\right)\prod_{\ell=1}^{f_{i}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;(y_{i,\ell}^{-1}u_{i})^{s};sm_{i}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(5.6)

where the contribution of a chiral multiplet with R-charge
R
is given by (
2.4
).

Theory (
5.1
) can be realised on D3-brane on a circle along with various fivebranes. There are several branches of the moduli space that can be obtained from the limit of the index, as explained in the preceding sections. In the following, we will describe another branch of the moduli space, known as the
geometric branch
.

Geometric branch

The geometric branch is obtained by considering

\begin{split}\phi_{i}=\phi~,\quad\text{for all $i=1,\ldots,n$}~.\end{split}

(5.7)

For simplicity, we set
f_{i}=0
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
for now. We will discuss the case in which some or all
f_{i}
are non-zero later.
We also assume that
A_{i}
,
B_{i}
are non-zero on this branch. Varying
W_{\text{eff}}
with respect to
A_{i}
(or
B_{i}
) and dividing by
B_{i}
(or
A_{i}
), we obtain the
F
-terms:

\begin{split}&(k^{-1}_{i}+k^{-1}_{i+1})A_{i}B_{i}-k^{-1}_{i+1}A_{i+1}B_{i+1}-k^{-1}_{i}A_{i-1}B_{i-1}=0~.\end{split}

(5.8)

As pointed out in
(
42
, (2.36), (2.6))
, the
F
-terms and
D
-terms corresponding to (
5.8
) can be written together as
48
48

48

Here, our matrix
{\cal M}
corresponds to
M^{\text{gen}}
in
(
42
, (2.36))
.

\begin{split}0=\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\cal M}_{ij}{\cal S}^{\dagger}_{j}\sigma_{\alpha}{\cal S}_{j}~,\end{split}

(5.9)

where
\sigma_{\alpha}
are the Pauli matrices, and

\begin{split}{\cal M}_{ij}=\begin{cases}k_{i}^{-1}+k_{i+1}^{-1}&\text{if $j=i$}\\
-k_{i+1}^{-1}&\text{if $j=i+1$}\\
-k_{i}^{-1}&\text{if $j=i-1$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}~.\end{split}

(5.10)

As pointed out in
(
42
, (2.36))
, we can also write
{\cal M}_{ij}
as
{\cal M}_{ij}=\sum_{a}k_{a}^{-1}X_{ai}X_{aj}
, where the index
a
runs over the nodes and the index
i
over the hypermultiplets, and
X_{ai}
is the charge matrix such that has value
1
if
A_{i}
is ingoing to the node
a
,
-1
if it is outgoing from it, and
0
otherwise. The rank of the matrix
{\cal M}
is
n-2
, provided that the sum of the Chern–Simons levels is zero
42
as in (
5.3
). Indeed,

\begin{split}{\cal M}\mathbf{\beta}^{t}=0~,\quad\text{with}\,\,\,\,\beta=\begin{pmatrix}1&1&\cdots&1\\
p_{1}&p_{2}&\cdots&p_{n}\end{pmatrix}~,\end{split}

(5.11)

where
p_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}k_{j}
, so that
k_{j}=p_{j}-p_{j-1}
. In fact,
(1,p_{1})
,
(1,p_{2})
,
\ldots,

(1,p_{n})
are the fivebranes involving in the brane realisation of theory (
5.1
) with
f_{i}=0
. It was shown in
42
that the geometric branch is isomorphic to the hypertoric manifold

\begin{split}\mathbb{H}^{n}///\ker(\beta)~.\end{split}

(5.12)

The authors of
28
generalised this result to the case in which some or all of the
f_{i}
are non-zero. In the corresponding brane configurations, there are
F
D5-branes partitioned as in (
5.2
). In which case, the
quantum corrected
geometric branch is described by

\begin{split}\mathbb{H}^{n+1}///\ker(\widetilde{\beta})~,\quad\text{with}\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\beta}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1&\cdots&1&0\\
p_{1}&p_{2}&\cdots&p_{n}&F\end{pmatrix}~.\end{split}

(5.13)

The set of holomorphic functions on (
5.13
) contains chiral fields
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
appropriately dressed with such monopole operators.

The Hilbert series as a limit of the index

The Hilbert series of the geometric branch of this quiver can be computed from the limit of the index of the theory with the following axial fugacity assignments:

1_{k_{1}}
1_{k_{2}}
1_{k_{3}}
1_{k_{4}}
1_{k_{5}}
1_{k_{n}}
f_{1}
f_{2}
f_{3}
f_{4}
f_{5}
f_{n}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a
a
a
a
a
a
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}

(5.14)

where the quiver is depicted in the 3d
{\cal N}=2
notation, and each bidirectional arrow between the circular nodes denotes the chiral multiplets
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
, whereas that between a circular node and a square node represents
Q_{i}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{i}
.

The corresponding index (
5.6
) is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.9}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{CSquivflvgeoma}}(x;a;w,v)\\
&=\left[Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;a^{-2};0\right)\right]^{n-2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\,\,\oint\frac{du_{1}}{2\pi u_{1}}\cdots\frac{du_{n}}{2\pi u_{n}}\,\,\prod_{i=1}u_{i}^{k_{i}m}w_{i}^{m}\\
&\quad\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;a(bu_{i}u_{i+1}^{-1})^{s};s(m_{i}-m_{i+1})\right)\prod_{\ell=1}^{f_{i}}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1/2}\left(x;a^{-1}(u_{i})^{s};sm_{i}\right)~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(5.15)

where we identify
u_{n+1}\equiv u_{1}
. The factor
\left[Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;a^{-2};0\right)\right]^{n-2}
is the contribution of the Lagrange multipliers (or “flipping fields”) that impose the
(n-2)
independent relations
0=\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\cal M}_{ij}{\cal S}^{\dagger}_{j}\sigma_{\alpha}{\cal S}_{j}
, where we recall that
(n-2)
is equal to the rank of the matrix
{\cal M}
. We denote these
(n-2)
Lagrange multipliers in (
5.14
) by the loops at every circular node, except at
\U(1)_{k_{1}}
and
\U(1)_{k_{n}}
.

Let us substitute (
2.13
) in the index (
5.15
). The power of
\mathfrak{z}
is

\begin{split}|m_{1}-m_{2}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+\ldots+|m_{n-1}-m_{n}|~.\end{split}

(5.16)

To obtain a finite result in the limit
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
(while keeping
t
fixed), the above expression must be zero. This puts the following constraint on the magnetic fluxes:

\begin{split}m_{1}=m_{2}=\ldots=m_{n}\equiv m\in\mathbb{Z}~,\end{split}

(5.17)

in agreement with (
5.7
). The dimension of the monopole operator is

\begin{split}&\Delta(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\left(|m_{1}-m_{2}|+|m_{2}-m_{3}|+\ldots+|m_{n-1}-m_{n}|+\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}|m_{i}|\right)~,\end{split}

(5.18)

where, with the flux configuration (
5.17
), we have

\begin{split}\Delta(m,\ldots,m)=F|m|~.\end{split}

(5.19)

In the limit
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
, the index becomes the following Hilbert series:

\begin{split}H(t,v,w)&=\text{PE}\left[-(n-2)t^{2}\right]\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}w^{m}t^{F|m|}~\\
&\quad\times\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\oint\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{i}^{k_{i}m}\right)\text{PE}\left[t\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(b\frac{u_{i}}{u_{i+1}}+b^{-1}\frac{u_{i+1}}{u_{i}}\right)\right]~,\end{split}

(5.20)

where
w=\prod_{j=1}^{n}w_{j}
is the fugacity for the diagonal combination of the topological symmetries associated to each gauge group. This is in agreement with
(
22
, (6.18))
. Note that
b
and
w
correspond to the two Cartan elements of the isometry of the geometric branch. The terms in the plethystic exponential in the second line are the contributions of
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
, and their associated flavour fugacities are
v
and
v^{-1}
. The factor
t^{F|m|}
is the dimension of the monopole operator given by (
5.19
). The factor
\text{PE}\left[-(n-2)t^{2}\right]
corresponds to the
(n-2)
independent
F
-terms given by the rank of the matrix
{\cal M}
discussed above. Observe that the chiral fields
Q_{i}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{i}
do not appear in the chiral ring. Only the sum
F
of the number of flavours contributes to the dimension of the monopole operators.

When
k_{i}=0
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
, the theory has a manifest
{\cal N}=4
supersymmetry, and its geometric branch is simply
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
, since the theory can be realised on a single M2-brane probing such a space in this case
52
. Other explicit examples can be found in
22
.

5.2
Comments on general non-Abelian circular quivers

In the previous subsection, we have considered the case where all gauge groups of (
5.14
) are Abelian. We now analyse the theories with non-Abelian gauge groups.

{N}_{k_{1}}
{N}_{k_{2}}
{N}_{k_{3}}
{N}_{k_{4}}
{N}_{k_{5}}
{N}_{k_{n}}
f_{1}
f_{2}
f_{3}
f_{4}
f_{5}
f_{n}

(5.21)

To begin, we consider the special case where all the CS-levels are set to zero, namely
k_{i}=0\,\forall\,i=1,\ldots,n
, and take the values fo the
f_{i}
parameters to be generic as long as
F=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}\neq 0
(the case of
F=0
is commented in
17
). This is the case of a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory whose brane setup consist of only NS5 and D5 branes. This provides a simpler setup to study the problem and obtain a result that, as we will comment later in the subsection, holds also for generic
k_{i}
levels.

We focus on the case where all gauge groups are
\U(2)
. The index reads

\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{N=2}(x;\{w_{i}\},\{v_{i}\},\{y_{i,l}\})\\
&=\frac{1}{(2!)^{n}}\sum_{\{m_{1}^{(\alpha)}\},\ldots,\{m_{n}^{(\alpha)}\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\oint\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du_{1}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{1}^{(\alpha)}}\ldots\frac{du_{n}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{n}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}^{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}m_{i}^{(\alpha)}}\frac{1}{[Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2},0)]^{2}}\\
&\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z^{\U(2)}_{\text{vec}}(x;\{u_{i}^{(\alpha)}\};\{m_{i}^{(\alpha)}\})\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2}u_{i}^{(\alpha)}(u_{i}^{(\beta)})^{-1};m_{i}^{(\alpha)}-m_{i}^{(\beta)})\\
&\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}\left(x;a(bu_{i}^{(\alpha)})^{s}(u_{i+1}^{(\beta)})^{-s};sm_{i}^{(\alpha)}-sm_{i+1}^{(\beta)}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\rho=1}^{F}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-1}(f_{\rho}^{-1}u_{i}^{\alpha})^{s};sm_{i}^{(\alpha)})~,\end{split}

(5.22)

with periodic parameters such that
\bm{u}_{n+1}\equiv\bm{u}_{1}
and
\bm{m}_{n+1}\equiv\bm{m}_{1}
. Notice that, in the second line, we divide by the contribution of two chiral multiplets with axial charge
-2
. This is conventional and is part of the prescription that we propose to compute the geometric branch. The computation that we will present will depend very little on this factor.
It is also part of the prescription that we assign axial charge
-1
to all the adjoint chiral fields and flavours while we assign charge
+1
to each bifundamental. This choice is instead crucial for the computation that will follow.

The geometric branch is expected to arise as the limit of the index

H_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}[\text{geom}](t)=\lim_{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{\cal I}_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}\Big|_{\eqref{N=3reparam}}~.

(5.23)

In the limit of
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
only the flux sectors carrying zero power of
\mathfrak{z}
contribute. The power of
\mathfrak{z}
reads

-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\alpha>\beta}^{2}|m_{i}^{(\alpha)}-m_{i}^{(\beta)}|+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}|m_{i}^{(\alpha)}-m_{i+1}^{(\beta)}|~,

(5.24)

which is a non-negative quantity for any set of fluxes
\textbf{m}_{i}
and is zero if and only if
\textbf{m}_{1}=\ldots=\textbf{m}_{n}\equiv\bf{m}
.
49
49

49

Indeed the fluxes can be equal up to permutations of their components. However, in the following, we pick a fixed Weyl chamber such that
m_{i}^{(\alpha)}\geq m_{i}^{(\beta)}
if
\alpha<\beta
for any
i=1,\ldots,n
. Therefore, we do not need to take into account permutations in the computation that follows.
Since all the magnetic fluxes are identified, the limit does not depend on the FI parameters of all the nodes but only on their diagonal combination
w=\prod_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}
.
The limit yields

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}(t;w;b)=\sum_{m_{1}>m_{2}}t^{F(|m_{1}|+|m_{2}|)}w^{m_{1}+m_{2}}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}+\sum_{m}t^{2F|m|}w^{2m}\frac{A_{2}(t,b)}{1-t^{2}}~,\end{split}

(5.25)

where the first contribution comes from the situation where
m^{(1)}=m_{1}\neq m^{(2)}=m_{2}
, thus each
\U(2)
gauge group is broken to
\U(1)\times\U(1)
, while the second contribution comes from the case
m^{(1)}=m^{(2)}=m
, which preserves each
\U(2)
gauge group. In (
5.25
), we defined the contribution

\begin{split}A_{N}(t,b)=&\frac{1}{(N!)^{n}}\oint\prod_{\alpha=1}^{N}\frac{du_{1}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{1}^{(\alpha)}}\cdots\frac{du_{n}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iu_{n}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{1\leq\alpha\neq\beta\leq N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{u_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{u_{i}^{(\beta)}}\right)\\
&\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b\left(\frac{u_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{u_{i+1}^{(\beta)}}\right)^{s}-t^{2}\left({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{N}\frac{u_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{u_{i}^{(\beta)}}\right)\right]~,\end{split}

(5.26)

in a such way that
A_{1}(t,b)
is the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}
:

\begin{split}A_{1}(t,b)=H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}}(t,b)=\mathop{\rm PE}[t^{2}+(b+b^{-1})t^{n}-t^{2n}]~.\end{split}

(5.27)

The term
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-1}
, highlighted in
red
, deserves some explanations as follows. First of all, its presence renders
A_{1}(t,b)
the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, as it should be for the Higgs branch of a Kronheimer-Nakajima quiver. Secondly, upon substituting (
5.26
) into (
5.25
), we see that each term in the latter contains the factor
\mathop{\rm PE}\left[2\,t^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}
, which is in agreement with the limit of the term
1/[Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2},0)]^{2}
in (
5.22
).

For
N>1
the quantity
A_{N}
is related to
A_{1}
. For example, the relations enjoyed by
A_{2,3}
are given by

\begin{split}&A_{2}(t,b)=\frac{1}{2(1-t^{2})}\times\left[(1-t^{2})^{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}+(1-t^{4})\times A_{1}(t^{2},b^{2})\right]\,,\\
&A_{3}(t,b)=\frac{1}{6(1-t^{2})}\big[(1-t^{2})^{3}\times A_{1}(t,b)^{3}+2(1-t^{4})(1-t^{2})\times A_{1}(t,b)A_{1}(t^{2},b^{2})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,+3(1-t^{6})\times A_{1}(t^{3},b^{3})\big]~.\end{split}

(5.28)

These relations, and those for higher
N
, have been found first in
17
(see the analogous relations also in
(
35
, (2.1), (2.5))
), from the analysis of the
F=0
case.

Going back to (
5.25
), we perform a couple of manipulations. For the first term, we split the sum into two independent summations over
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
, which requires to introduce the missing contributions of
m_{1}=m_{2}
in the sum. By doing that we are led to

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}(t;w;b)&=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}\sum_{m}t^{F|m|}w^{m}\right)^{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}\\
&\quad+\sum_{m}t^{2F|m|}w^{2m}\left[\frac{A_{2}(t,b)}{1-t^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}\right]~,\end{split}

(5.29)

where, in the first line, we also multiplied and divided by
(1-t^{2})^{2}
, so that we recognise that the sum yields the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
, namely

\begin{split}H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t;w)=\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}\sum_{m}t^{F|m|}w^{m}=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t^{2}+(w+w^{-1})t^{F}-t^{2F}\right]~.\end{split}

(5.30)

We then use the relation for
A_{2}
in (
5.28
) to get

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}(t;w;b)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}\sum_{m}t^{F|m|}w^{m}\right)^{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}\\
&\quad\,\,+\sum_{m}\frac{1}{1-t^{4}}t^{2F|m|}w^{2m}\left[\frac{(1-t^{2})^{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}+(1-t^{4})A_{1}(t^{2},b^{2})}{2(1-t^{2})^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-t^{2}}\sum_{m}t^{F|m|}w^{m}\right)^{2}A_{1}(t,b)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{4})^{2}\sum_{m}\frac{1}{1-t^{4}}t^{2F|m|}w^{2m}A_{1}(t^{2},b^{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\Big[H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t,w)^{2}H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}}(t,b)^{2}+\frac{(1+t^{2})^{2}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t^{2},w^{2})H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}}(t^{2},b^{2})\Big]~.\end{split}

(5.31)

We then recognise the contribution of the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
to write

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{indcircN=4}}(t;w;b)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\left[H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t;w;b)^{2}+\frac{(1+t^{2})^{2}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t^{2};w^{2};b^{2})\right]~.\end{split}

(5.32)

Notice that the factor
\frac{(1+t^{2})^{2}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}=\mathop{\rm PE}[4t^{2}-2t^{4}]
is a deviation from the formula being equal to the symmetric product of two
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
spaces (up to a prefactor), each of which is the geometric branch in the Abelian case. In particular, the formula for the Hilbert series for such a symmetric product is

\begin{split}&H_{\mathrm{Sym}^{2}(\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F})}(t;w;b)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\left[H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t;w;b)^{2}+H_{\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}}(t^{2};w^{2};b^{2})\right]~.\end{split}

(5.33)

Using relations of the form in (
5.28
), it is quite straightforward to generalise this result to the case of
\U(N)
gauge groups to find a similar deviation from the symmetric product of
N

\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
spaces.

To conclude, we now comment on the generalisation of this result for the case of circular quivers with non-zero CS levels, thus to brane setups including other type of fivebranes besides D5 and NS5. The steps leading to (
5.25
) are identical, the difference is that the
A_{N}
contribution now depends on the values of the magnetic fluxes due to the presence of CS levels. This makes the computation quite more difficult and we will not attempt to carry it out. However, we propose that the formula in (
5.32
) should hold for any quiver by replacing
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
with the geometric branch of the Abelian case:

H_{N=2}(t;w;b)=\frac{1}{2}(1-t^{2})^{2}\left[H_{N=1}(t;w;b)^{2}+\frac{(1+t^{2})^{2}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}H_{N=1}(t^{2};w^{2};b^{2})\right]~.

(5.34)

A direct check of this proposal is given in the example discussed below.

5.2.1
Circular quiver (
3.126
)

In this subsection, we consider the geometric branch of the circular quiver (
3.126
) with
N=2
and
k=F=1
. This can be computed in a similar way to (
3.127
) and (
3.128
), but with the following modifications. First of all, the factor

\begin{split}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i}u^{\alpha}_{i}(u^{\beta}_{i})^{-1};m^{(\alpha)}_{i}-m^{(\beta)}_{i}\right)\end{split}

(5.35)

in (
3.127
) should be further divided by
Z_{\text{chir}}^{1}\left(x;\mathfrak{b}_{i};0\right)^{2}
to remove the trace of the adjoint chiral multiplet for each gauge group. Secondly, the parameters
\mathfrak{b}_{i}
should be taken as
\mathfrak{b}_{i}=a^{-2}
(with
i=1,2
) for the geometric branch, whereas the other parameters are taken as the last line of (
3.128
). These modifications are parts of the prescription and are necessary to obtain the limit of the index that contains a contribution from the global symmetry current
\mathfrak{su}(3)
, which is a global symmetry for the theory corresponding to
N^{0,1,0}
.

On the geometric branch, the magnetic fluxes of each gauge node are equal and take the form

\begin{split}\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n})~,\end{split}

(5.36)

where, as usual, we can use the
\U(2)
Weyl group to set
\mathfrak{m}\geq\mathfrak{n}
. The limit of the index receives two contributions, one from
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}
and the other from
\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}
:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivN010}_{N=2,k=F=1}}[\text{geom}]=H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}+H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}~.\end{split}

(5.37)

Similarly to (
3.27
), for fluxes with
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}
, the limit of the index is

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.88}{$\begin{split}&H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{1}{2^{2}}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{i})^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}w_{i}^{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\Bigg)_{\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m})}t^{2F|m|}\\
&\times\prod_{1\leq\alpha\neq\beta\leq 2}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\Bigg(1-\frac{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{u^{(\beta)}_{i}}\Bigg)\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s,\sigma=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}\left(b^{\sigma}\frac{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}}{u^{(\beta)}_{2}}\right)^{s}-t^{2}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}\frac{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{u^{(\beta)}_{i}}\right)\right]~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(5.38)

where
\kappa_{1}=-\kappa_{2}=k>0
, and the term
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-2}
in takes into account of the tracelessness of the adjoint chiral multiplet for each gauge group. This is in agreement with the discussion below (
5.26
). The power
2F|m|
of
t
in the first line comes from two times (
3.137
) with
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}
. For reference, we report the result for the unrefined Hilbert series for
H_{\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{n}}
(with
b=w_{1}=w_{2}=1
) as follows:

\begin{split}1+4t^{2}+18t^{4}+46t^{6}+109t^{8}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(5.39)

For fluxes with
\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}
, similarly to (
3.29
), the limit of the index is

\begin{split}H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\,{\cal H}(\mathfrak{m})\,{\cal H}(\mathfrak{n})\,\,t^{F(|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{n}|)}\\
&=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}\,\frac{(w_{1}w_{2})^{\mathfrak{m}+\mathfrak{n}}}{(1-t^{2})^{2}}\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}=0}^{\infty}[k|\mathfrak{m}|+2p_{1}]_{b}[k|\mathfrak{n}|+2p_{2}]_{b}\,t^{(k+F)(|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{n}|)+2p_{1}+2p_{2}}~,\end{split}

(5.40)

where
{\cal H}(m)
is the integral part of the
N=1
case, given in the third line of (
3.133
) with
w=w_{1}w_{2}
:

\begin{split}{\cal H}(m)&=\frac{(w_{1}w_{2})^{m}}{1-t^{2}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}[k|m|+2p]_{b}\,t^{k|m|+2p}~,\end{split}

(5.41)

and the power
F(|\mathfrak{m}|+|\mathfrak{n}|)
of
t
in the first line comes from two times (
3.137
) with
\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}
.
For reference, we report the result for
H_{\mathfrak{m}>\mathfrak{n}}
(with
b=w_{1}=w_{2}=1
) as follows:

\begin{split}4t^{2}+30t^{4}+132t^{6}+409t^{8}+\ldots~.\end{split}

(5.42)

Summing (
5.38
) and (
5.40
), we obtain the following result written in terms of the character of
\mathfrak{su}(3)
representations as in (
3.134
), with the subscripts
b
and
w
omitted for brevity:

\begin{split}H_{\eqref{quivN010}}[\text{geom}](t;b;w)=1&+[1,1]t^{2}+(2[2,2]+2-[1,1])t^{4}\\
&+(2[3,3]+[4,1]+[1,4]-[3,0]-[0,3])t^{6}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(5.43)

where we use the fugacity map as stated in Footnote
24
. Due to the fact that this result contains terms with negative coefficients, the limit of the index in this case cannot be interpreted as a Hilbert series, since the latter counts gauge invariant quantities parametrising the moduli space. Note that we can also derive (
5.43
) directly from formula (
5.34
) simply by taking
H_{N=1}(t;w;b)
to be (
3.134
). This confirms the consistency between the analysis in this subsection and that in the previous one.

It is instructive to compare this with the Hilbert series of the second symmetric power of the closure of the minimal nilpotent orbit of
\mathfrak{su}(3)
, which is the expected geometric branch of this theory:

\begin{split}&H\left[\mathrm{Sym}^{2}(\overline{\min\,\mathfrak{su}(3)})\right](t;b;w)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}[n,n]_{b,w}t^{2n}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}[n,n]_{b^{2},w^{2}}t^{4n}\right]\\
&=1+[1,1]t^{2}+(2[2,2]+[1,1]+1)t^{4}\\
&\quad\,\,\,\,\,+(2[3,3]+[4,1]+[1,4]+2[2,2]+[3,0]+[0,3]+[1,1])t^{6}+\ldots~,\end{split}

(5.44)

where we drop the subscripts
b,w
in the last equality.

5.3
Affine
\mathcal{N}=4

D_{N}
quiver

The prescription for studying the geometric branch can also be extended to general affine quivers, beyond the
A_{N}
type. In particular, in this section, we study the geometric branch of the
\mathcal{N}=4

D_{N}
affine quiver with an arbitrary number of flavours attached to each node of the quiver. The theory is realised by the following quiver:

1
1
1
1
2
2
\cdots
2
F_{1}
F_{2}
F_{4}
F_{3}
F_{5}
F_{6}
F_{N+1}

(5.45)

For convenience, we refer to the gauge nodes attached to
F_{j}
as the
j
-th gauge node. As usual, we denote by
X_{ij}
the chiral field in the bifundamental hypermultiplet going from the
i
-th gauge node to the
j
-th gauge node. The theory can be obtained by a single M2-brane probing
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{N}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
; see
52
, with
F
given by

F=F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}+F_{4}+2\sum_{i=5}^{N+1}F_{i}~.

(5.46)

Hence,
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{N}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
is the expected geometric branch of the theory. We confirm this prediction with the explicit computation of the Hilbert series.
To compute the various limits of the index, we first assign axial fugacities to the chiral fields. In 3d
{\cal N}=2
notation, the field content and fugacity assignments are

1
1
1
1
2
2
\cdots
2
F_{1}
F_{2}
F_{4}
F_{3}
F_{5}
F_{6}
F_{N+1}
a
a
a
a
a
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-1}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}
a^{-2}

(5.47)

where we omit two adjoint chirals in the vector multiplets of two
\U(1)
gauge groups, as part of the prescription to compute the limit of the index for the geometric branch.
As the result will not depend on the specific choice of the values
F_{i}
, let us consider the simplified case where
F_{1}=F
, while every other
F_{i}=0
. The index for the theory (
5.47
) is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.95}{$\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\eqref{affineD4axial}}(x;\{y_{i}\},\{w_{i}\})=\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{N-3}}\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\bm{n}_{1},\ldots,\bm{n}_{N-3}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\oint\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{du_{i}w_{i}^{m_{i}}}{2\pi iu_{i}}\prod_{i=5}^{N+1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{dv^{(\alpha)}_{i}w_{i}^{n_{i}^{(\alpha)}}}{2\pi iv^{(\alpha)}_{i}}Z^{\U(N)}_{\text{vec}}(x;\bm{v}_{i};\bm{n}_{i})\\
&\quad\times\left[Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2};0)\right]^{2}\prod_{i=5}^{N+1}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2}v_{i}^{(\alpha)}(v_{i}^{(\beta)})^{-1};n_{i}^{(\alpha)}-n_{i}^{(\beta)})\\
&\quad\times\prod_{i=5}^{N}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}\left(x;a(v_{i}^{(\alpha)})^{s}(v_{i+1}^{(\beta)})^{-s};sn_{i}^{(\alpha)}-sn_{i+1}^{(\beta)}\right)\\
&\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\Bigg[Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}\left(x;au_{i}^{s}(v_{5}^{(\alpha)})^{-s};sm_{i}-sn_{5}^{(\alpha)}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\,\times Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}\left(x;au_{i+2}^{s}(v_{N+1}^{(\alpha)})^{-s};sm_{i+2}-sn_{N+1}^{(\alpha)}\right)\Bigg]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{\rho=1}^{F}\prod_{s=\pm 1}Z^{1/2}_{\text{chir}}\left(x;a^{-1}u_{1}^{s}(y^{(\rho)})^{-s};sm_{1}\right)~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(5.48)

where, in the third line, the factor
\left[Z^{1}_{\text{chir}}(x;a^{-2};0)\right]^{2}
corresponds to two chiral multiplets with R-charge one and fugacity
a^{-2}
under the two
\U(1)
gauge groups attached to
F_{3}
and
F_{4}
.
To compute the geometric branch, as usual, we redefine

x=\mathfrak{z}t~,\quad a=\mathfrak{z}^{-1/2}t^{1/2}~,

(5.49)

and then perform the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit.
A non-vanishing result in the
\mathfrak{z}\to 0
limit imposes the condition that the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
must be zero. In this case, the exponent of
\mathfrak{z}
is given by

\begin{split}&-\sum_{i=5}^{N+1}|n_{i}^{(1)}-n_{i}^{(2)}|+\sum_{i=5}^{N}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}|n_{i}^{(\alpha)}-n_{i+1}^{(\beta)}|+\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{2}(|m_{i}-n_{3}^{(\alpha)}|+|m_{i+1}-n_{N+1}^{(\alpha)}|)+F|m_{1}|~.\end{split}

(5.50)

In the limit, the following conditions are imposed on the fluxes in the theory:

\begin{split}m_{i}=m~,\quad\bm{n}_{i}=(m,m)~.\end{split}

(5.51)

The limit of the index for the geometric branch is then given by

\begin{split}&H_{\eqref{affined4}}[\text{geom}](t;y)=\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{F|m|}y^{m}}{1-t^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{N-3}\oint\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}\prod_{i=5}^{N+1}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{dv_{i}^{(\alpha)}}{2\pi iv_{i}^{(\alpha)}}(1-t^{2})^{2}\\
&\quad\times\prod_{i=5}^{N+1}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\left(1-(v_{i}^{(1)})^{s}(v_{i}^{(2)})^{-s}\right)\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-t^{2}(v_{i}^{(\alpha)})^{s}(v_{i}^{(\beta)})^{-s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{i=5}^{N}\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t(v_{i}^{(\alpha)})^{s}(v_{i+1}^{(\beta)})^{-s}\right]\\
&\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[tu_{i}^{s}(v_{5}^{(\alpha)})^{-s}+tu_{i+2}^{s}(v_{N+1}^{(\alpha)})^{-s}\right]~,\end{split}

(5.52)

where we redefine the topological fugacity as

y=w_{1}w_{2}w_{3}w_{4}\prod_{i=5}^{N+1}w^{2}_{i}~.

(5.53)

We then recognise that the integral, which does not depend on the magnetic flux, evaluates to the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{N}
, while the sum over fluxes yields the Hilbert series of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
. Thus the geometric branch Hilbert series is that of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{N}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
.

Let us discuss this in further details, focusing on the case of
N=4
for simplicity. The generators of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{F}
are given by

\begin{split}G_{0}=\mathop{\rm tr}(X_{15}X_{51})=\mathop{\rm tr}(X_{25}X_{52})~,\qquad G_{\pm}=V_{\pm(1,1,1,1,(1,1))}~,\end{split}

(5.54)

satisfying
G_{+}G_{-}=G_{0}^{2F}
. The equalities on the left follow from the
F
-terms associated with the adjoint chiral in the vector multiplet of the
\U(2)
gauge group, and
\mathop{\rm tr}
denotes the trace over the fundamental representation of such a gauge group. On the other hand, the generators of
\mathbb{C}^{2}/\widehat{D}_{4}
, along with their relation, are explicitly given in
(
48
, (3.4), (3.7), Figure 3)
.

We discuss the geometric branch of
D_{4}
affine Dynkin quivers with Chern-Simons levels in Appendix
C
. It is straightforward to generalise the above analysis to
E_{N}
affine Dynkin quivers. Furthermore, we anticipate that rank-two—and more generally higher-rank—
D_{N}
affine Dynkin quivers will admit a treatment analogous to that presented for the
A_{N}
case in Section
5.2
.

Appendix A
Some useful identities

In this Appendix, we collect various identities used in the main text.

For linear quivers, we apply the following identity for the
\U(1)_{\kappa}\times\U(1)_{-\kappa}
gauge group with a bifundamental hypermultiplet and gauge magnetic fluxes
(m,m)
:

\begin{split}\oint\frac{du}{2\pi iu}\frac{dv}{2\pi iv}w^{m}u^{\kappa m}v^{-\kappa m}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}(buv^{-1})^{s}\right]=\frac{t^{|\kappa||m|}}{1-t^{2}}(b^{-\kappa}w)^{m}~.\end{split}

(A.1)

Note that the factor
(1-t^{2})^{-1}
is actually the
P
-factor
P_{\U(1)}(t;m)
introduced in
21
.

Observe that identity (
A.1
) can be generalised, for example, to a linear quiver containing the
\U(2)_{\kappa}\times\U(2)_{-\kappa}
gauge group and a bifundamental hypermultiplet. We consider the gauge magnetic fluxes
\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(m_{1},m_{2})
. There are two contributions: one from the case of
m_{1}=m_{2}
(each
\U(2)
gauge factor is preserved), and the other from the case of
m_{1}\neq m_{2}
(each
\U(2)
gauge factor is broken to
\U(1)^{2}
):

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.92}{$\begin{split}&\oint\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{i}}(u^{(\alpha)}_{i})^{\kappa_{i}m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}w_{i}^{m^{(\alpha)}_{i}}\Bigg)_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{m}_{1}=\bm{m}_{2}=(m_{1},m_{2})\\
\kappa_{1}=-\kappa_{2}=\kappa\end{subarray}}\\
&\quad\times\Bigg\{\delta_{m_{1},m_{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\prod_{1\leq\alpha\neq\beta\leq 2}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\Bigg(1-\frac{u^{(\alpha)}_{i}}{u^{(\beta)}_{i}}\Bigg)\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}(bu^{(\alpha)}_{1})^{s}(u^{(\beta)}_{2})^{-s}\right]\\
&\qquad\quad+(1-\delta_{m_{1},m_{2}})\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\sum_{s=\pm 1}\sum_{\alpha=1,2}(bu^{(\alpha)}_{1})^{s}(u^{(\alpha)}_{2})^{-s}\right]\Bigg\}\\
&=t^{|\kappa|\sum_{i}|m_{i}|}\,\,P_{\U(2)}(t;m_{1},m_{2})\,\,(b^{-\kappa}w_{1}w_{2})^{\sum_{i}m_{i}}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(A.2)

where
P_{\U(2)}(t;m_{1},m_{2})
is defined as in
(
21
, (A.2))
:

\begin{split}P_{\mathrm{U}(2)}(t;m_{1},m_{2})=\begin{cases}\left[(1-t^{2})(1-t^{4})\right]^{-1}&\qquad m_{1}=m_{2}\\
(1-t^{2})^{-2}&\qquad m_{1}\neq m_{2}\end{cases}~.\end{split}

(A.3)

Moreover, throughout the paper, we consider various limits of the index. For convenience, we collect the contributions of a chiral field with R-charge
R
, whose expression is given by (
2.4
), to the index in these limits below:

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.93}{
\begin{tabular}[]{l|c|c}\hline\cr Chiral field&Limit $\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0$&Limit $t\rightarrow 0$\\
\hline\cr$Z^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\text{chiral}}(x;az,m)$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}z^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\delta_{m,0}tz\right]$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}z^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$\\
$Z^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\text{chiral}}(x;a^{-1}z,m)$&$\left(tz^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$&$\left(tz^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\delta_{m,0}\mathfrak{z}z\right]$\\
$Z^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\text{chiral}}(x;z,m)$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}t\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{4}}z^{-\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}t\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{4}}z^{-\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$\\
$Z^{1}_{\text{chiral}}(x;a^{2}z,m)$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}t^{-1}z^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\delta_{m,0}t^{2}z\right]$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}t^{-1}z^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-\delta_{m,0}\mathfrak{z}^{2}z^{-1}\right]$\\
$Z^{1}_{\text{chiral}}(x;a^{-2}z,m)$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}^{-1}tz^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[-\delta_{m,0}t^{2}z^{-1}\right]$&$\left(\mathfrak{z}^{-1}tz^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\delta_{m,0}\mathfrak{z}^{2}z\right]$\\
$Z^{1}_{\text{chiral}}(x;z,m)$&$z^{-\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$&$z^{-\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}
}\end{split}

(A.4)

Detailed computation of Section

The

T[\U(2)]
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}y
\times\,\ T[\U(1)]\,\ \equiv

(B.1)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)}_{\text{adj}}(\mathfrak{z},t)&\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}1-t^{2}~,\end{split}

(B.3b)

Let us also analyse the contribution coming from the hypermultiplet in the bifundamental representation of

, which, using (

) and (
