), is given by

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.93}{$\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t)&=\mathfrak{z}^{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|l_{z}-{\bm{m}_{f}}_{i}\right|}\\
&\times\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1-(-1)^{sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}}\mathfrak{z}^{2+\left|sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}\right|+2j}t^{1+\left|sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}\right|+2j}z^{-s}f_{i}^{s}}{1-(-1)^{sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}\right|+2j}t^{1+\left|sl_{z}-s{m_{f}}_{i}\right|+2j}z^{s}f_{i}^{-s}}~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(B.4)

In the limits
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
and
t\rightarrow 0
, the expression above simplifies to
50
50

50

Observe that a single chiral field of R-charge
\frac{1}{2}
, whose expression is given in (
2.4
), behaves as
Z^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\text{chiral}}(\mathfrak{z},t;z,m)\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}\delta_{m,0}\frac{1}{1-tz}
,
Z^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\text{chiral}}(\mathfrak{z},t;z,m)\overset{t\rightarrow 0}{=}\left(\mathfrak{z}z^{-1}\right)^{\frac{\left|m\right|}{2}}
, see
53
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t)\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1}{1-tz^{s}f_{i}^{-s}}~,\end{split}

(B.5a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t)\overset{t\rightarrow 0}{=}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}~.\end{split}

(B.5b)

It follows that, in the case
{m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2}
, the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory admits the following expression in the limit
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
:

\begin{split}&Z_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z},t;{m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2})\\
&\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}\sum_{l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(1-t^{2}\right)\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\prod_{s=\pm 1}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1}{1-tz^{s}f_{i}^{-s}}\\
&=\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{2{m_{f}}_{1}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1-t^{2}}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{2}}{z}\right)}~.\end{split}

(B.6)

Upon computing the residues at the poles of the gauge fugacity
z
, this yields the Hall-Littlewood formula for the Hilbert series of
T[\U(2)]
, as defined in
(
19
, Section 3.1.2)
,
51
51

51

Actually, in that reference, the Hall-Littlewood formula is provided for the
T[\mathrm{SU}(2)]
theory. Here, instead, we are dealing with the
T[\U(2)]
theory, hence an extra term
\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}
, which is absent in the Hall-Littlewood formula for
T[\mathrm{SU}(2)]
, appears. As a further comment, observe that we we can rewrite
\frac{1}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}
in (
B.8
) as
\left(1-t^{2}\right)^{2}K^{\U(2)}_{[1^{2}]}(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2})
, where

\begin{split}K^{\U(2)}_{[1^{2}]}(t;x_{1},x_{2})=\mathop{\rm PE}\left[t\chi^{\U(2)}_{\text{adj}}(x_{1},x_{2})\right]=\prod_{1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2}\frac{1}{1-tx_{\alpha}x^{-1}_{\beta}}\end{split}

(B.7)

is defined in
(
19
, (3.20))
.

where we take
{n_{w}}_{1}\geq{n_{w}}_{2}
:

\begin{split}Z_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z},t;{m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2})&\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}t^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\,\ \times\frac{1}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)~,\end{split}

(B.8)

where the expression for the
\U(2)
Hall-Littlewood polynomial is given by
(
19
, (B.13))

\begin{split}\Psi^{(n_{1},n_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t;x_{1},x_{2}\right)=\frac{tx_{1}^{1+n_{2}}x_{2}^{n_{1}}-x_{1}^{n_{2}}x_{2}^{1+n_{1}}+x_{1}^{1+n_{1}}x_{2}^{n_{2}}-tx_{1}^{n_{1}}x_{2}^{1+n_{2}}}{x_{1}-x_{2}}~.\end{split}

(B.9)

If
{n_{w}}_{1}<{n_{w}}_{2}
, it is sufficient to swap
{n_{w}}_{1}\leftrightarrow{n_{w}}_{2}
in the equation (
B.8
). Let us now consider the case
{m_{f}}_{1}\neq{m_{f}}_{2}
in the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit. Since (
B.5a
) vanishes, we have to study the leading order in
\mathfrak{z}
, i.e. the expression with the lowest non-zero power of
\mathfrak{z}
which appears in the limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory . As can be observed from (
B.5b
), given that we sum over
l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}
, the leading order in
\mathfrak{z}
is reached when
{m_{f}}_{\text{min}}\equiv\min\{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}\}\leq l_{z}\leq\max\{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}\}\equiv{m_{f}}_{\text{max}}
, for which the power of
\mathfrak{z}
is
{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}
. For instance, if we focus on the contribution coming from
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}
, the hypermultiplet in the bifundamental representation of
\U(1)\times\U(2)
behaves as

\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t;l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1})\overset{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}{=}\left[\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}\right]\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}~,\end{split}

(B.10)

then the expression for the
T[\U(2)]
theory in the
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}\neq{m_{f}}_{2}
sector reads

\begin{split}\sum_{l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}}&\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\times\left(1-t^{2}\right)\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\\
=&\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{1}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{2}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\frac{1-t^{2}}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\\
=&\left[w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{1}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{1}\right)\right]\left[w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{2}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{2}\right)\right]\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}~,\end{split}

(B.11)

where
\Psi^{(n)}_{\U(1)}\left(x\right)=x^{n}
. This is just the product of the Hall-Littlewood formulae of two
T[\U(1)]
theories, multiplied by an overall factor
\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}
.
Similarly, the contribution coming from the
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{2}
sector can be obtained immediately from (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) by swapping the pair
(f_{1},{m_{f}}_{1})
with
(f_{2},{m_{f}}_{2})
. Finally, we also have to consider the
{m_{f}}_{\text{min}}<l_{z}<{m_{f}}_{\text{max}}
sectors, which contribute to the limit as

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.86}{$\begin{split}&\sum_{l_{z}={m_{f}}_{\text{min}}+1}^{{m_{f}}_{\text{max}}-1}\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(1-t^{2}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\\
&=\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(1-t^{2}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\,\ \sum_{i=1}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|-1}\left[w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{\text{min}}+i}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{1}\right)\right]\left[w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{\text{max}}-i}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{2}\right)\right]~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(B.12)

The expressions (
B.11
) and (
B.12
), together with the one for the
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{2}
contribution to the limit, can be also written in a more meaningful way by manifestly distinguishing the two cases in which the background fluxes
{n_{w}}_{1}
and
{n_{w}}_{2}
either differ or take the same value. In particular, in the latter case, the said expressions can be incorporated by reformulating the various terms of the type
w^{{m_{f}}_{i}}_{1}w^{{m_{f}}_{j}}_{2}
in terms of
\U(2)
Hall-Littlewood polynomials as functions of
\mathfrak{z}^{2}
,
(w_{1},w_{2})
and the background fluxes
({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})
. In such a way, taking into account also (
B.8
), we finally obtain the complete expression for the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory, which reads

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.77}{$\begin{split}&Z^{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z};t|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})=\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{\Theta({n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2})\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)+\Theta({n_{w}}_{2}-{n_{w}}_{1}-1)\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2},{n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad+\left(1-\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{{m_{f}}_{2}}\right|}\Bigg\{\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{2}}{w_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\Big(\Theta({m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2})\Big[\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)-t^{2}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1}-1,{m_{f}}_{2}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\Big]\\
&\qquad\qquad\,\,\ +\Theta({m_{f}}_{2}-{m_{f}}_{1}-1)\Big[\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{2},{m_{f}}_{1})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)-t^{2}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{2}-1,{m_{f}}_{1}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\Big]\Big)\\
&\qquad\quad+\left(1-\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\right)t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}\sum_{i\neq j=1}^{2}\left[w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{i}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{i}\right)\right]\left[w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{j}}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(f_{j}\right)\right]\Bigg\}~,\end{split}$}\end{split}

(B.13)

where we denote by
\Theta(x)
the Heaviside step function, such that
\Theta(x)=1
for
x\geq 0
and
\Theta(x)=0
for
x<0
.
Let us comment on the result (
B.13
). As already pointed out around (
B.8
), when the two magnetic fluxes associated with the
\U(2)
flavour symmetry are equal, i.e.
{m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2}
, the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index reproduces the Hall-Littlewood formula of the
T[\U(2)]
theory. On the other hand, when the two magnetic fluxes
{m_{f}}_{1}
and
{m_{f}}_{2}
take different values, the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory yields a sum of various terms. Explicitly, when
{n_{w}}_{1}={n_{w}}_{2}
, these can be expressed in terms of
\U(2)
Hall-Littlewood polynomials in the variables
\mathfrak{z}
,
(w_{1},w_{2})
and
({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})
, which give rise to a modified
T[\U(2)]
Hall-Littlewood-type formula which is proportional to a positive power of
\mathfrak{z}
, appearing from the third to the fifth line in (
B.13
). On the other hand, when both
{m_{f}}_{1}\neq{m_{f}}_{2}
and
{n_{w}}_{1}\neq{n_{w}}_{2}
, then the various terms appearing in the last line of the above limit of the
T[\U(2)]
limit correspond to the product of two
T[\U(1)]
couplings.

B.2
The
t\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of
T[\U(2)]

Let us now consider the
t\rightarrow 0
limit of the
T[\U(2)]
theory. Taking into account (
B.3b
) and (
B.5b
), this reads

\begin{split}&\sum_{l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}}\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)}\\
&=\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\sum_{l_{z}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{1}^{l_{z}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{1}+{m_{f}}_{2}-l_{z}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)}~.\end{split}

(B.14)

The infinite series in the expression above can be resummed in closed form thanks to the following identities, valid for
{m_{f}}_{1}\geq{m_{f}}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}+1}^{+\infty}\frac{\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)}&=\frac{\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{1+{m_{f}}_{1}}\mathfrak{z}^{2+{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)}~,\end{split}

(B.15a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{l_{z}={m_{f}}_{2}}^{{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)}&=\frac{w_{1}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}-w_{2}\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{2}}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)}\mathfrak{z}^{{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}}~,\end{split}

(B.15b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{l_{z}=-\infty}^{{m_{f}}_{2}-1}\frac{\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{l_{z}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{1}\right|+\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)}&=\frac{\left(\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)^{-1+{m_{f}}_{2}}\mathfrak{z}^{2+{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}}}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{2}}{w_{1}}\right)}~.\end{split}

(B.15c)

Upon summing together the contributions (
B.15a
)–(
B.15c
), the expression (
B.14
) for
{m_{f}}_{1}\geq{m_{f}}_{2}
reduces to

\begin{split}\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{1}}\mathfrak{z}^{{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}}\frac{1}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{2}}{w_{1}}\right)}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)~,\end{split}

(B.16)

whereas, in the case
{m_{f}}_{2}>{m_{f}}_{1}
, it is sufficient to swap
{m_{f}}_{1}\leftrightarrow{m_{f}}_{2}
in the expression above. Observe that this reproduces exactly (
B.8
) upon exchanging
(t;\bm{f},\bm{m}_{f})
with
(\mathfrak{z};\bm{w},\bm{n}_{w})
, in agreement with the self-mirror property of the
T[\U(2)]
theory.
52
52

52

The
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
and
t\rightarrow 0
limits of the
T[\U(2)]
theory, as defined in (
2.13
) with axial fugacity
y=a
in (
B.1
), coincide with the Higgs and Coulomb branch limits, respectively, introduced in
(
53
, (3.37))
. Since the
T[\U(2)]
SCFT enjoys self-mirror symmetry, the two limits coincide.
Since (
B.16
) vanishes when
{n_{w}}_{1}\neq{n_{w}}_{2}
, in such a case we have to isolate the terms appearing with the lowest power of
t
, which contribute when the gauge flux
l_{z}
takes one of the following values:
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}\neq{m_{f}}_{2}
,
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{2}\neq{m_{f}}_{1}
, and
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2}
. For instance, in the
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}\neq{m_{f}}_{2}
sector, we consider
53
53

53

Let us comment on the difference between the expressions (
B.17
) and (
B.10
). The quantity inside the square bracket in (
B.17
) is
\frac{1+{\cal O}(\mathfrak{z}^{2}t)}{1+{\cal O}(t)}
, which reduces to
\frac{1}{1+{\cal O}(t)}
when
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
, which explains why the numerator is equal to one in (
B.10
). On the other hand, in (
B.17
), since we are interested in the lowest order in
t
, the numerator and the denominator are of the type
1+{\cal O}(t)
, hence we have to keep them both.

\begin{split}Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t;l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1})\overset{t\rightarrow 0}{=}\left[\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}\right]\mathfrak{z}^{\left|l_{z}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}~,\end{split}

(B.17)

then the corresponding contribution to the
t\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory reads

\begin{split}&\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}w_{1}^{{m_{f}}_{1}}w_{2}^{{m_{f}}_{2}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}}\frac{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\\
&=\frac{\left[f_{1}^{{n_{w}}_{1}}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(w_{1}\right)\right]\left[f_{2}^{{n_{w}}_{2}}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(w_{2}\right)\right]\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t^{2}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)}~.\end{split}

(B.18)

Analogously, the contribution coming from the
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{2}\neq{m_{f}}_{1}
sector can be achieved by swapping
(f_{1},{m_{f}}_{1})
with
(f_{2},{m_{f}}_{2})
in both (
B.17
) and (
B.18
). Finally, when
l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2}
, the
\U(1)\times\U(2)
bifundamental hypermultiplet contributes as

\begin{split}&Z^{\U(1)\times\U(2)}_{\text{hyper}}(\mathfrak{z},t;l_{z}={m_{f}}_{1}={m_{f}}_{2})\\
&\overset{t\rightarrow 0}{=}\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\delta_{l_{z},{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{2}}{z}\right)}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{2}}{z}\right)}\end{split}

(B.19)

to the limit. For such value of the gauge flux
l_{z}
, the
t\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory with
{n_{w}}_{1}\geq{n_{w}}_{2}
then reads

\begin{split}&\oint\frac{dz}{2\pi iz}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}z^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{1}{1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}}\frac{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{z}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t\frac{f_{2}}{z}\right)}{\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{1}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{1}}{z}\right)\left(1-t\frac{z}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t\frac{f_{2}}{z}\right)}\\
&=\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}t^{{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}}\frac{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t^{2}\right)}{\left(1-t^{2}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad\times\Big[\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1}-1,{n_{w}}_{2}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)\\
&\qquad-\mathfrak{z}^{2}t^{2}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1}+1,{n_{w}}_{2}-1)}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)+\mathfrak{z}^{4}t^{2}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)\Big]~,\end{split}

(B.20)

whereas, for
{n_{w}}_{2}\geq{n_{w}}_{1}
, it is sufficient to swap
{n_{w}}_{1}\leftrightarrow{n_{w}}_{2}
in the expression above. From (
B.16
), (
B.18
) and (
B.20
), we can finally report a closed formula for the
t\rightarrow 0
limit of the index of the
T[\U(2)]
theory, which can be written compactly as

\begin{split}\scalebox{0.8}{$\begin{split}&Z^{t\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z},t|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})=\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\left(f_{1}f_{2}\right)^{{n_{w}}_{1}}\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\\
&\qquad\times\frac{\Theta({m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2})\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)+\Theta({m_{f}}_{2}-{m_{f}}_{1}-1)\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{2},{m_{f}}_{1})}_{\U(2)}\left(\mathfrak{z}^{2};w_{1},w_{2}\right)}{\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{1}}{w_{2}}\right)\left(1-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\frac{w_{2}}{w_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad+\left(1-\delta_{{n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2}}\right)t^{\left|{n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2}\right|}\Bigg\{\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\left(w_{1}w_{2}\right)^{{m_{f}}_{1}}\frac{1}{\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right)\left(1-t^{2}\frac{f_{2}}{f_{1}}\right)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\Big(\Theta({n_{w}}_{1}-{n_{w}}_{2})\Big[\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1},{n_{w}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{1}-1,{n_{w}}_{2}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(t^{2}\right)\Big]\\
&\qquad\qquad\,\,\ +\Theta({n_{w}}_{2}-{n_{w}}_{1}-1)\Big[\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2},{n_{w}}_{1})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)-\mathfrak{z}^{2}\Psi^{({n_{w}}_{2}-1,{n_{w}}_{1}+1)}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};f_{1},f_{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(t^{2}\right)\Big]\Big)\\
&\qquad\quad+\left(1-\delta_{{m_{f}}_{1},{m_{f}}_{2}}\right)\mathfrak{z}^{\left|{m_{f}}_{1}-{m_{f}}_{2}\right|}\sum_{i\neq j=1}^{2}\left[f_{1}^{{n_{w}}_{i}}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{1})}_{\U(1)}\left(w_{i}\right)\right]\left[f_{2}^{{n_{w}}_{j}}\Psi^{({m_{f}}_{2})}_{\U(1)}\left(w_{j}\right)\right]\Bigg\}~.\end{split}$}\end{split}

(B.21)

Observe that the limit above coincides with (
B.13
) upon exchanging
\left(t;\bm{f},\bm{m_{f}}\right)
with
\left(\mathfrak{z};\bm{w},\bm{n_{w}}\right)
, in agreement with the self-mirror property of the
T[\U(2)]
theory:

\begin{split}Z^{\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(\mathfrak{z};t|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w})=Z^{t\rightarrow 0}_{T[\U(2)]}(t;\mathfrak{z}|\bm{w};\bm{n}_{w}|\bm{f};\bm{m}_{f})~.\end{split}

(B.22)

B.3
Comments on various magnetic flux configurations

Below (
3.74
), we discuss the nontrivial contributions of the following configurations of magnetic fluxes:

1.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}\}
: the only non-trivial contributions come from the sectors with
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}\}
.

2.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{1},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{2}\}
: the only non-vanishing contributions come from the sectors with
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}_{2}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}_{2}\}
.

3.

\{m^{(1)}_{2}=m^{(2)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{1},m^{(2)}_{2}=m^{(1)}_{3}=\mathfrak{m}_{2}\}
: this choice of magnetic fluxes is analogous to case
2
, to which is related simply by permuting
m^{(1)}_{3}
and
m^{(2)}_{3}
.

We now provide the arguments for the trivial contribution of the magnetic fluxes
\{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
and
\{m^{(\alpha)}_{4}\}
that do not satisfy the above conditions.

Point
1
.

If one considers the monopole sectors with
m^{(1)}_{1}\neq m^{(2)}_{1}
instead, the only contributions of the gauge fugacities
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
appearing in the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of (
3.74
) are the the integration measure
\prod\limits_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{1}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{1}}
, the terms containing the Chern-Simons couplings
(u^{(1)}_{1})^{\kappa_{1}m^{(1)}_{1}}
and
(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\kappa_{1}m^{(2)}_{1}}
, as well as the factor
\left(u^{(1)}_{1}u^{(2)}_{1}\right)^{\mathfrak{m}}
coming from the
T[\U(2)]
block connecting the
\U(2)
nodes with gauge fugacities
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
and
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{2}\}
. The integrand picks up a residue in
u^{(1)}_{1}=0
for
\mathfrak{m}=-\kappa_{1}m^{(1)}_{1}
, but there is no residue in
u^{(2)}_{1}=0
since
\mathfrak{m}\neq-\kappa_{1}m^{(2)}_{1}
in these sectors. Analogous arguments also hold when
m^{(1)}_{4}\neq m^{(2)}_{4}
.

Point
2
.

The magnetic fluxes satisfying
\{m^{(1)}_{1}=m^{(2)}_{1}=\mathfrak{l}\}
,
\{m^{(1)}_{4}=m^{(2)}_{4}=\mathfrak{n}\}
do not contribute to the
\mathfrak{z}\rightarrow 0
limit of the index. This statement can be explained by focusing, for instance, just on the gauge fugacities
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
, where we can assume
\mathfrak{m}_{1}>\mathfrak{m}_{2}
. These fugacities appear in (
3.74
) due to the contributions coming from the integration measure
\prod\limits_{\alpha=1}^{2}\frac{du^{(\alpha)}_{1}}{2\pi iu^{(\alpha)}_{1}}
, the Chern-Simons terms
(u^{(1)}_{1}u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}}
, the factor
2-\frac{u^{(1)}_{1}}{u^{(2)}_{1}}-\frac{u^{(2)}_{1}}{u^{(1)}_{1}}
associated with the leftmost
\U(2)
vector multiplet in (
3.61
), as well as the contribution associated with the
T[\U(2)]
block on the left, namely
\Psi^{({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2})}_{\U(2)}\left(t^{2};u^{(1)}_{1},u^{(2)}_{1}\right)\times\mathop{\rm PE}\left[\sum\limits_{s=\pm 1}t^{2}(u^{(1)}_{1})^{s}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{-s}\right]
. Upon parametrising
\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}+\mathfrak{m}_{1}=\mathfrak{x}
and
\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{l}+\mathfrak{m}_{2}=\mathfrak{y}
, with
\mathfrak{x}>\mathfrak{y}
, the whole
\{u^{(\alpha)}_{1}\}
-dependence of the integrand in (
3.74
) can be expressed compactly as

\begin{split}\left(u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1}\right)\left[\frac{(u^{(1)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{x}-1}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{y}-1}}{t^{2}u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1}}+\frac{(u^{(1)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{y}-1}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{x}-1}}{u^{(1)}_{1}-t^{2}u^{(2)}_{1}}\right]~.\end{split}

(B.23)

Two different possibilities must be taken into account here.

•

Let us consider the situation in which there are no poles in
u^{(1)}_{1}=0
first. This happens if
\mathfrak{y}\geq 1
, which also implies
\mathfrak{x}\geq 2
. In such a case, the computation of the residue in
u^{(1)}_{1}=t^{2}u^{(2)}_{1}
yields a contribution coming from the gauge fugacity
u^{(2)}_{1}
with power
\mathfrak{x}+\mathfrak{y}-1
, which is always positive. Consequently, there is no residue to be taken with respect to
u^{(2)}_{1}
.

•

Next, let us focus, for instance, on the quantity

\begin{split}\frac{(u^{(1)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{y}-1}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{x}}}{u^{(1)}_{1}-t^{2}u^{(2)}_{1}}~,\end{split}

(B.24)

which possesses a pole of order
1-\mathfrak{y}
in
u^{(1)}_{1}=0
. The computation of the residue at such pole yields a negative term
-t^{2\mathfrak{y}-2}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{x}+\mathfrak{y}-1}
, which perfectly cancels the result coming from the computation of the residue in
u^{(1)}_{1}=t^{2}u^{(2)}_{1}
, that is
t^{2\mathfrak{y}-2}(u^{(2)}_{1})^{\mathfrak{x}+\mathfrak{y}-1}
. The total contribution then cancels, meaning that the integral vanishes also in this case.
